# LACTOSE Verification

What must be measured or re-checked before a milestone can rely on it. `docs/architecture.md`
is the binding design and its milestone table (section 22) holds the proof criteria; this file
lists the spikes, the third-party claims and the open items those milestones depend on, each
with the milestone that gates it.

## 1. Spikes

| Spike | What it records | Gates milestone |
|---|---|---|
| io_uring on CI runners | Whether io_uring is available on the CI runners | 0. **Done:** yes on the Linux runner. The nightly's io_uring probe (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml`) creates a ring on `ubuntu-latest`, kernel 7.0.0-1019-azure on x86_64 with `io_uring_disabled` 0, in nightly 43408989655 at `d7827ea` and nightly 43581924303 at `ca65ec9`, so `lactose-io` can test an io_uring backend in CI |
| Clippy on primitive methods | Whether `disallowed-methods` matches inherent primitive methods on the pinned toolchain |0. **Done:** on 1.99.0 it matches both `a.min(b)` and `f32::max(a, b)` forms, and `f64::sin`, `to_bits`, `powi` and `clamp` |
| Scalar precision | Simulation scalar precision at full map extent: the walk scene for 36,000 ticks on upstream 32-bit Rapier, at the origin and translated by 6 km and by 12 km, against the same pairs in 64 bits as the control, differences taken in f64; the result file carries the probe. The 32-bit branch is built only if the error stays under one millimetre (`docs/plans/milestone-1.md`, decision 5.4) | 1. **Done:** 32 bits fails, 64 bits stays (`docs/results/scalar-precision-apple-m1-2026-11-14.md`, Apple M1). The probe closes the walk's square, since run 1's open square walked the walker off the floor and failed the control, and checks that every run stays on its floor. The 64-bit control stays at or under 0.032 mm on every pair: the scene's own sensitivity in y to the jump landings, the same at 6 km and 12 km. 32 bits reaches 157.75 mm at (6000, 0, 6000) and 94.38 mm at (6000, 0, 0), in x and z, and 88.14 mm and 34.07 mm before the walker first comes within the 2 cm prediction distance of a block; 12 km, past the 8,192 m binade boundary and informative only, reaches 120.84 mm without coming within it. The error is a speed bias of the f32 position sum, not a random walk, and the square's retracing path undoes it each lap; a straight 600-tick walk without jumps drifts 6.1 mm per metre walked at 6 km and 13.7 mm per metre at 12 km, so the bias grows with distance walked and the square's figures are lower bounds. By architecture section 16 the 32-bit branch is not built and the rule's SOUR half is moot. Decided (D146): the scalar is 64-bit. The rule is read at x = 6 km, D59's figure, since no document states the map's extent; 8 km lies in 6 km's binade and was not read; 64 bits is measured to 12 km. The plan's cost condition is moot, since it was written for a probe that passed, and a shallow ring is `lactose-predict`'s question at milestone 4 (the row "The frame re-simulation budget has no figure"). A 32-bit scalar comes back only with a design that keeps simulation coordinates small, run against the rule again |
| MoltenVK and KosmicKrisp | Indirect-draw behaviour, encode time for ten thousand draws, pipeline creation cost, memoryless attachments and local reads | 2. **In part, MoltenVK 1.4.2 on the M1** (`docs/results/spike-rhi-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`, headless): no lazily allocated memory for an image with input-attachment usage; a local read ends the Metal render pass with stores and begins one with loads; a memoryless attachment loses its contents across that restart; `drawIndirectCount` unsupported. Encode time, pipeline creation cost and KosmicKrisp are open. Both drivers run on the M1 once it is on macOS 27.2.1 (D154): KosmicKrisp needs Metal 4, so macOS 26 or later, and on macOS 14.7.4 the SDK's driver enumerated no device. Lanes `vulkan` and `swapchain` record the capability answers under each, from the conformance suite and the swapchain's cases, and result file `gpu-cost` the encode time and the pipeline creation cost, through `rhi-vulkan`. **The suite under both, on the M1** (branch `vulkan`, `cargo xtask gpu` on the M1, both drivers (macOS 27.2.1, Vulkan SDK 1.4.363.1 and its Khronos layer, core, synchronisation with `syncval_load_op_after_store_op_validation`, and thread-safety validation, every listed canary heard or skipped with its reason, the one across queues skipped under KosmicKrisp, where compute shares the graphics queue; and under MoltenVK Metal API and shader validation as well, both banners printed by every case that makes a device and did not skip, the 44 skipped cases exempt, 14 of them after making a device, and Metal's report of a read past a buffer heard by its canary; the suite at 175 cases, 147 contract and 28 output)): MoltenVK 1.4.2 reports multi-draw indirect, no `drawIndirectCount`, a lazily allocated type for depth and none for colour, coherent host memory, compute on a queue of its own (four families of one queue, each drawing) and present shared, and passes every case it runs (150 passed, 44 skipped by decision 5.10's rule, 0 failed, 27 of the 28 output cases, all but the count draw); KosmicKrisp (SDK 1.4.363) reports multi-draw indirect and `drawIndirectCount`, no lazily allocated type, coherent host memory, compute and present sharing the graphics queue, and passes every case it runs (143 passed, 50 skipped, 0 failed, 27 of the 28 output cases, the count draw among them, all but the ownership transfer, which needs a compute queue of its own). The counts are of the device tests, the suite's 175 cases and the device's own. Both report viewport bounds of -16384 to 16383. A memoryless attachment across a local read is reported lost on both, as no query answers it. Encode time and pipeline creation cost are `gpu-cost`'s. **The encode time and the pipeline creation cost, in part** (branch `measure-m2`, round two and its fix round; not the record): `cargo xtask measure gpu-cost` under each driver, every layer off, the record on a thread named `record` and the submit on the test's, the render pass's GPU time by `lactose-rhi-vulkan`'s `Timestamps`, each written in an encoder submitted alone on either side of the pass's submit, the layout its device test holds to a lagging render pass under both drivers. In the lane's fix-round `--all` run on the M1 (commit 7e3b62a, after fix rounds one and two): under MoltenVK 1.4.2 (load averages 3.74 4.61 5.84 before and 3.68 4.58 5.82 after), the medians of 10,000 indexed draws as draws record 269 µs, submit 666.8 µs, GPU 1,670.9 µs; as one multi-draw record 2.4 µs, submit 728.1 µs, GPU 1,798.8 µs; as one indirect draw over the compacted buffer record 2 µs, submit 50 µs, GPU 369.9 µs; the render pass with its clear alone GPU 377.7 µs, what the pair's handoffs between submits read with no draw; no round's GPU time past the host's round trip; the timestamps' valid bits 64; 96 pipelines source-cold 13.8 ms each (0 stage hits, 192 misses), specialisation-cold 2.5 ms each (192 stage hits, 0 misses), and from the reloaded cache of 1,500,043 bytes 0.1 ms each (192 stage hits, 0 misses); under KosmicKrisp vulkan-sdk-1.4.363 (load averages 3.04 4.22 5.57 before and 2.96 4.19 5.55 after), the medians of 10,000 indexed draws as draws record 1,820.8 µs, submit 54.2 µs, GPU 1,302.1 µs; as one multi-draw record 910.5 µs, submit 59 µs, GPU 1,093.5 µs; as one indirect draw over the compacted buffer record 214.9 µs, submit 58.6 µs, GPU 380.7 µs; the render pass with its clear alone GPU 305.5 µs, what the pair's handoffs between submits read with no draw; no round's GPU time past the host's round trip; the timestamps' valid bits 64; 96 pipelines source-cold 27.9 ms each (0 stage hits, 192 misses), specialisation-cold 13.8 ms each (0 stage hits, 192 misses), and from the reloaded cache of 835,897 bytes 0.1 ms each (192 stage hits, 0 misses). The pair's figure is the pass with the queue's handoffs between the three submits; round two's layout, which this one replaces, let MoltenVK latch its second write inside a render pass (review r1's G1), and its run read the draws' pass at 828.5 µs under MoltenVK. Cold means cold in the Vulkan pipeline cache: Metal's per-user shader cache sits below it, and under KosmicKrisp the feedback's hit bits cannot tell cold from warm there, so the specialisation-cold set is new to it only as its 96 values, used whole by both stages and salted for the run from 32 bits of `RandomState`'s hasher, are unlikely to have been drawn before; and the source-cold set is cold because each run salts its source anew. The set's fast reading in round two's run (0.2 ms each) was, by review r1's G2, of values an earlier run had compiled, the salt then the clock's microseconds and the constant used modulo 256 in one stage. The quiet window's files, and the desktop's, close the row's figures |
| Extract cost | Per-dirty-row extract cost; the workload and machines of D153, result file `extract-50k`. The scene presents 5,000 of its rows, half of them moved, their keys interleaved across chunks: criterion 5's count, the most presented rows a criterion of section 22 names, though of another kind (D164). Criterion 4 is read at that scene once lane `snapshot-order` has merged; a figure taken on the fill that sorts every column at every pass is steering, and the file's figure at 10,000 presented rows is recorded, not judged | 2. **In part** (branch `measure-m2`, round one and its fix round; not the record): `cargo xtask measure extract-50k` runs `extract::cost_of_extract_at_50k_dirty_rows` of `lactose-app-client`'s `integration` target, section 4's workload with 10,000 presented rows, the measuring lane's choice pending the planner (the plan names no count), on the device and on null, each pass timed inside its bounded future. In the lane's fix-round `--all` run on the M1 (commit 7e3b62a, after fix rounds one and two) under MoltenVK 1.4.2, at load averages 4.61 5.10 6.21 before and 4.48 5.07 6.19 after: the pass's median on 4 workers 1,669.5 µs on the device and 1,660.1 µs on null; on one worker 2,388 µs on the device, of it the ring alone 1,295.1 µs, 25.9 ns a dirty row; the ring's part 236.8 µs and the snapshot's 1,094.5 µs, each a difference of two medians; no wait for a free region. The figure a dirty row is the ring's alone: the snapshot part copies every presented row on every pass after a tick, serially, whatever the dirty count, so it is a cost of the presented count and not of the dirty rows. The median is over criterion 4's 1 ms, as in each of the lane's runs. No run is the quiet window's, whose file, with the desktop's, closes the row. **Read at the plan's scene** (lane `snapshot-order`, after D164's fill; steering, not the record): `cargo xtask measure extract-50k` at commit f22485f on the M1 under MoltenVK 1.4.2, no layer, the ring in memory type 1 of 3 (DEVICE_LOCAL + HOST_VISIBLE + HOST_COHERENT + HOST_CACHED), 60 passes an arm after a full one, at load averages 9.64 8.97 6.39 before and 9.11 8.88 6.37 after (the lane's run, under the GPU lock, not the record): at 5,000 presented rows on 4 workers the pass's median 670.9 µs on the device, of it the ring's part 176.2 µs and the snapshot's 92.5 µs, and 680.8 µs on null, the snapshot's part 109.6 µs; on one worker 1,421.2 µs on the device, the ring alone 1,321.4 µs, 26.4 ns a dirty row; no wait for a free region. Beside it on null, on 4 workers, not judged: at 10,000 presented rows the pass 768.0 µs, the ring alone 585.4 µs, the snapshot's part 182.7 µs; and a pass that sorts, one presented row despawned and another spawned every tick, 782.7 µs against the ring alone's 568.8 µs, the snapshot's part 213.8 µs. The median is under criterion 4's 1 ms on the device at that load; the quiet window's file, with the desktop's, closes the row |
| Prediction-world snapshot cost | Full-copy cost per tick. Decided (D139, architecture section 10): copy-on-write is not built, and a prediction world holds at most 64 fixed colliders and proxies together as rows; what milestone 1 still measures is the slot through `lactose-predict` with Rapier, proxies included, which confirms the count or changes it. Measured by `crates/predict/tests/cost.rs` (World and physics snapshots, 50 claimed bodies plus 0 to 10,000 statics, test backend) and the backend lane's Rapier scene, `Ring::bytes` for the size; the depth `app` derives from it is what makes a correction cost no lead (D122), and `DEFAULT_DEPTH` is a placeholder until then. The facade review measured a full-copy `World::snapshot` alone at 1.3 to 2.9 µs and 18.8 KB with no statics, 14.5 to 19 µs and 262 KB with 1,000, 134 to 149 µs and 2.43 MB with 10,000 (test profile, load 1.2 to 2.4): statics as rows are over the 100 µs budget before physics, so the count a prediction world holds as rows is what the measurement bounds. Measured by the implementation (lane predict-impl at `8a744fd`, Apple M1, release, test backend, load averages 8.70 15.57 15.74): world snapshot 87 kB / 5.4 µs, 390 kB / 28 µs and 3.06 MB / 333 to 380 µs, and physics snapshot 14.6 kB / 3.4 µs, 306 kB / 49 µs and 2.92 MB / 715 µs, for 0, 1,000 and 10,000 statics: with the test backend the budget holds to about 1,000 statics; with Rapier (the row "Physics costs on the milestone 1 ECS against the 3 ms physics phase") a slot's take holds to about 100 fixed colliders and a restore to about 75. The review's rerun (the same commit, Apple M1, load averages 7.15 20.93 21.78) agreed: 0 statics world 4.6 to 4.7 µs, physics 2.9 µs, predicted tick 74 to 80 µs, re-simulated 83 to 88 µs per tick. Result file `prediction-slot` (`cost_of_a_prediction_slot`, crates/app/tests/spike.rs), on the spike's thrown scene among 59 unclaimed bodies: the character, fifty claimed bodies, five fixed colliders and 59 kinematic proxies, the 64 D139 admits; the slot's sizes, 91,988 + 251,041 + 14,892 B, are asserted equal to the client ring's. Under agent load the snapshot ran 143 to 189 µs and the restore 199 to 267 µs; on the same scene without proxies they are 103 to 119 µs and 145 to 163 µs. The 59 proxies add about 0.6 µs each to the take and 0.9 µs each to the restore, near D139's 0.6 and 1.1; the rest is the fifty claimed bodies, chiefly their contact pairs, so the count of rows does not alone keep a take inside the budget; a quiet-machine run decides. The earlier estimate of about 100 fixed colliders for the take and 75 for the restore no longer holds: it counted no claimed body's contact state. Open: a cheaper collider-set copy than the encode, decode and swap of `shapes_out` / `shapes_in`, or a lighter narrow-phase image, would act on both terms. After the ECS fork's merge, under agent load: snapshot 151.5 µs (world 5.8, physics 145.7), restore 205.2 µs. Quiet window (the byte form's first, `1d8042e:docs/results/prediction-slot-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`, load averages 1.51 2.09 3.69 before and after): snapshot 140.8 µs (world 5.6, physics 135.2), record 54.4 µs, restore 193.0 µs (world 12.3, physics 180.6): criterion 5 is not met, and the restore is over section 10's figure. The planner's probe at `1d8042e` (`docs/plans/criterion-5-proposal.md`, section 2; the calmest of ten runs, load averages 6.83 15.67 16.88, physics snapshot 138.4 µs) splits the backend's write of 133.7 µs: the narrow phase 48.5 µs, which is the claimed bodies' 263 contact pairs, a manifold each; the collider set 36.0 µs, 27.2 µs of it the encode, decode and exchange of `shapes_out`; the bodies 24.6 µs; the broad phase 11.2 µs; the islands 6.6 µs; the bookkeeping 4.5 µs. The 59 proxies add 0.6 µs each, about 35 µs, as D139 estimated, and without one the probe put the same fifty bodies' snapshot at 106 µs under load, so no bound that admits the spike's scene brings the byte form inside the budget. Decided (D143): a slot holds the backend's world as a copy the backend makes, shapes shared, and the bytes stay for the fresh solver, the journal checkpoint and the hash. A copied slot costs its take and the drop of the slot it evicts. Under load, and so estimates: the planner's probe took a copy in 28.2 µs; the proposal's first review put a ring of sixty copies inside that probe (load averages 5.75 5.30 10.04) and took one in 29.3 to 29.9 µs and dropped the evicted one in 26.1 to 27.5 µs, so a copied slot of about 65 µs with the world's part, the world snapshot's own eviction not timed; a restore from a copy took 44.3 µs where the byte restore took 176.8 µs. The planner's probe re-simulated the spike's four scenes and its claim on approach to the byte restore's records over 1,800 ticks each, and the review's backend probe agreed at 409 restore points with joints, sleeping bodies and a replaced shape; a restore that kept the live pipeline diverged in 6 of 52. The budget stays 100 µs and the bound stays 64 until the copied slot is measured, taken into a full ring, in a second quiet window at the first commit that holds lane `slot-copy`. Since D143 the physics part of a slot is the backend's copy, and `Ring::bytes` reports the bytes the backend would write, not the memory a slot holds, which `crates/physics-rapier/tests/memory.rs` counts under the tagged allocator. Second quiet window (`docs/results/prediction-slot-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md` at `c50b688`, load averages 2.96 2.75 5.85), the copied slot taken into a full ring of 60, each part's eviction inside its timer (`cost_of_a_prediction_slot`): snapshot 90.0 µs (world 11.2, physics 78.9), the sum of its parts' medians over 200 rounds, record 61.5 µs, restore 76.5 µs (world 14.6, physics 62.0), each the median of 200 rounds: criterion 5 is met, read at those figures; no figure bounds a single take, and the bound stays 64 (D143's second addendum). **Done** (lanes `measure`, `spike-net` and `slot-copy`): the slot, its 59 proxies included, measured through `lactose-predict` with Rapier (`cost_of_a_prediction_slot`, `crates/app/tests/spike.rs`); copy-on-write is not built (D139) and the slot is the backend's copy (D143) | 1 |
| Native Metal | Argument buffers, residency, memoryless attachments, framebuffer fetch, indirect draws; `rhi-api` is frozen or amended on its findings; result file `spike-metal`, and the planner's freeze decision at milestone 2's exit (D153) | 2. **In part, the spike on the M1** (`docs/results/spike-metal-apple-m1-2026-11-17.md`, macOS 27.2.1, headless): argument buffers, residency, memoryless attachments, framebuffer fetch and indirect draws all yes, clean under Metal API and shader validation; the RHI spike's fourteen points hold, point 4 with the rule that a memoryless attachment is never stored, and samplers, aliased transients and the drawable are added; indirect command buffers stay out of `rhi-api`. Missing residency shows under shader validation; without it, as stale reads while its writer runs in some runs only, so a lagging-writer readback is not evidence of it. A binary archive is neither opened nor serialised under shader validation (a load segfaults, and one written under it misses every pipeline), and before the parse `rhi-metal` checks an identity beside it and the length and checksum of a private copy. Three conflicts with section 12.1, D45 and D43 are named for the freeze decision, which is owed; `rhi-api`'s merge amends section 12.1's two and takes a side on all three until then (D158) |
| Remote transform upload | Cost per frame for 5k interpolated entities at 144 Hz; the workload of D153, result file `remote-upload-5k`. Criterion 5 reads the frames whose job began and ended on a performance core, at whatever clock, in the foreground task role, with their share of the run; the figures over all frames and by clock band are recorded beside them, and where macOS starts the job is D164's placement row, not the criterion's | 2. **Steering only** (branch `presentation`, `crates/presentation/tests/cost.rs`, ignored, run in release on the Apple M1; not the result file, which is `measure-m2`'s): 5,000 remote rows of a client's replicated world, the schedule's frame (one `sample_batch` call, the merge with the meshes and the conversion) and the upload from the frame's job to a closure that counts the rows, paced at 144 Hz for 10 s with a plain thread pushing a state into every slot at 30 Hz. The sample's median varied between runs, from 0.26 to 1.8 ms, with where macOS ran the frame's job (below): paced at 144 Hz with no other core busy it was 1.8 ms (6 of 6 runs, `reviewer-concurrency`); with a core spinning beside it, or unpaced, 0.26 to 0.38 ms (7 of 7); in the branch's last runs, below, 0.39 to 0.45 ms. The batch call's figure is a second `sample_batch` call on the same slots made after the frame, not a share of the frame's time. The branch's last runs, with the frame handed the acquired pair: two runs as the test stands, at 1-minute load averages 3.32 then 3.26 (other agents' work on the machine): the frame's median 452.9 and 452.8 µs, 99th percentile 592.1 and 897.2 µs; of it the sample's median 449.8 and 449.7 µs and the upload's 3.4 and 3.8 µs; the second batch call's median 307.3 and 307.1 µs. Two runs with one core spinning beside it, at 3.21 then 4.11 (4.38 after): the frame's median 400.6 and 390.5 µs, 99th percentile 2,526.4 and 832.9 µs; the sample's median 394.3 and 385.9 µs; the second batch call's median 268.2 and 266.3 µs. The ring's copy is not in it while `RemoteWriter::write` is the facade's. `measure-m2` makes the recorded figure: it runs the writer under `ThreadClass::Network`, times the batch call inside the frame, and reports the reader's retry rate (row 102). **The result file's test** (branch `measure-m2`, round one and its fix round; not the record): `upload::cost_of_the_remote_upload_of_5k` of `lactose-app-client`'s `integration` target, the writer under `ThreadClass::Network`, and inside the frame's job the batch call, the conversion and the write to the device's ring through `RemoteWriter::write`, each timed, and before them a fixed chain of dependent `black_box` multiply-adds (5,000 steps) that classes each frame against the fastest chain seen: `full` under 1.25 times it, `reduced` under twice, `half` at twice or more, bands relative to that chain and not to a core's rated clock; beside the paced run, an unpaced control of the same frames back to back. The frame runs in two modes, and the paced median follows the share of frames in the slower: on a frame-critical worker (`USER_INTERACTIVE` QoS) macOS runs a light job paced at 144 Hz much of a run on an efficiency core or at a lower clock. `reviewer` and `reviewer-concurrency` read the core and the clock of each job in review r1, on null: in one run 990 of 1,440 jobs on an efficiency core at load 3.67, their frame median 1,854.2 µs against 573.2 µs on a performance core; across seven paced runs the jobs started on an efficiency core were 68 to 1,106 of 1,440, at loads from 4.92 to 14.89 in no order of load, and the slow runs' clock chain read about half the efficiency cluster's top frequency; unpaced, 1,387 of 1,440 ran on a performance core at 293.5 µs. In the lane's fix-round `--all` run on the M1 (commit 7e3b62a, after fix rounds one and two) under MoltenVK 1.4.2, at load averages 4.48 5.07 6.19 before and 4.32 5.02 6.16 after: paced, the frame's median 1,849.7 µs, P99 2,880.9 µs and worst 3,875.4 µs over 1,440 frames, of it the batch call's median 1,302.5 µs, the conversion's 398.7 µs and the write's 140.1 µs; by the chain, `full` 1.7% of the frames at a frame median of 298.1 µs (the batch call 212.7 µs, the conversion 45.7 µs), `reduced` 10.7% of the frames at a frame median of 578.6 µs (the batch call 463.8 µs, the conversion 79.7 µs) and `half` 87.6% of the frames at a frame median of 1,858.9 µs (the batch call 1,305.4 µs, the conversion 399.7 µs); unpaced, the frame's median 275 µs and P99 795.2 µs, `full` 95.1% of the frames at a frame median of 274.5 µs (the batch call 201.8 µs, the conversion 42.2 µs). The paced median is over criterion 5's 1 ms and its P99 over 2 ms, from the frames in the slower mode; the unpaced median is under 1 ms. Whether criterion 5 is judged as macOS places the job is the planner's. The file flags a run whose unpaced control has under half its frames `full` (`calibration.flag`): a fastest chain the busy control cannot hold, not a run slowed throughout (Low Power Mode, a thermal limit), which no band read against the run's own chains can tell. In the lane's run on the M1 after fix round three (commit 16f45fc, `frame-pace` and `remote-upload-5k` alone), at load averages 6.32 12.00 19.80 before and 6.30 11.80 19.64 after, it reads `no`, the control 95.2% `full`. **In the foreground task role** (D164; lane `measure-role`, item 5; steering, not the record): the test takes the role through `lactose_task::take_foreground_task_role`, the file reads it back (`task_role`), and each frame's job reads the class of the core it began on and the one it ended on through `lactose_task::current_core_class`. In three runs on the M1 under MoltenVK 1.4.2 the role read `TASK_FOREGROUND_APPLICATION` at user-interactive priority 47 and no paced frame's job began and ended on an efficiency core, where without the role 68 to 1,106 of 1,440 jobs started on one. At commit 3a67a04, at load averages 15.43 23.21 15.77 before and 16.06 23.10 15.81 after: paced, 1,417 of 1,440 frames (98.4%) began and ended on a performance core, their median 321.2 µs, P99 379.0 µs and worst 539.0 µs, 99.8% of them `full`; the other 23 (1.6%) began on one class and ended on the other, their median 334.4 µs; over every frame the median 321.3 µs, P99 386.7 µs and worst 552.1 µs, of it the batch call's median 229.1 µs, the conversion's 49.2 µs and the write's 42.6 µs; unpaced, 1,415 (98.3%) on a performance core at a median of 288.1 µs and P99 340.0 µs, the other 25 moved. The two runs before it (commit 408d1dd with the change uncommitted), at load averages 43.56 19.44 10.64 and 24.85 25.74 15.67 before and 43.58 28.38 15.34 and 27.62 26.37 16.08 after, read 98.8% and 98.7% of the paced frames on a performance core at medians of 349.2 and 364.1 µs, none on an efficiency core. The numbering the macOS class call rests on, shown on the machine: the fastest chain of 5,000 steps 13.7 µs on a performance core and 19.3 µs on an efficiency core, over the frames and the 3,200 chains of a numbering probe of one `Background` thread for each logical CPU, 1,187 of them on an efficiency core: the efficiency class's the slower, as the call assumes (`calibration.numbering`). An efficiency core at its fastest reads `reduced` by the chain (1.41 times the fastest), so a band does not tell a class. Since fix round three the file flags the numbering, and criterion 5 then judges nothing, unless the efficiency class's fastest chain is at least 1.05 times the performance class's (`calibration.numbering_ratio`): run 3's 1.41, a frame's chain on a performance core against the probe's on an efficiency core, passes it, and within the probe alone `reviewer-diff` read 1.13 to 1.14. The check catches a swap of the classes and a tie, not every wrong map; the real check of the numbering is `lactose-task`'s `the_apple_numbering_is_the_device_trees`. On a machine with one class of core criterion 5 reads every paced frame (`criterion.reads`). No run is the quiet window's, whose file, with the desktop's, closes the row |
| Physics phase share | The physics phase's share of the tick, on the composed tick of `cargo xtask measure` (the 100k cost scene with 2,000 awake bodies). Result file `composed-tick`. Quiet window (`docs/results/composed-tick-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`, load averages 1.43 2.09 3.71): T₁ 13,808.9 µs, of which physics 3,156.1 µs, 23%; T₄ 5,772.5 µs, of which physics 2,265.8 µs, 39%. It has no threshold (D139). The x86 desktop's file is session B's. **Done** (lane `measure`), as the milestone 1 plan closes it: the share measured on the composed tick (`composed::cost_of_the_composed_tick`, `crates/app/tests/cost.rs`, at `bef3455`); session B's file adds the desktop's figure beside it | 1 |
| Physics phase stall | The stall the physics phase imposes on render recording during the overlap; nothing records a frame before `render-graph` exists; result file `record-stall`, recorded with no threshold, sets `lactose-app`'s default reservation (D153). Until the file is read the default is one frame worker: `lactose_app::RECORD_RESERVATION`, which `WorldConfig::shown()` gives the physics configuration (D162). The file is taken at a reservation of one and of two, and on Windows it also gives the time from each `block_in_place`'s end to the record's next job on a worker that was parked, which is what the `SetEvent` for each frame worker in `Pool::open_gate` costs a record (the keeper's unmeasured list, read by D162) | 2. **In part** (branch `measure-m2`, round one and its fix round; not the record): `stall::the_record_beside_the_physics_phase` of `lactose-app-client`'s `integration` target records `ClientRender` on null, each record one `run_job` timed from the job's start, its start wait from `run_job`'s call beside it, beside the composed tick timed inside its bounded future; the physics phase is `physics_api::sync`, `step` and `write_back`, from the first entry into any to the last exit, matched by name in the executor's node spans and `lactose-ecs`'s system spans. In the lane's fix-round `--all` run on the M1 (commit 7e3b62a, after fix rounds one and two), at load averages 4.32 5.02 6.16 before and 4.53 5.05 6.16 after, 60 ticks at each reservation: at one, the record's median and P99 beside the physics phase 7.7 and 49.5 µs, their start wait's 1.1 and 20.8 µs, the phase's median 2,502.7 µs; at two, 7.6 and 43.3 µs, the start wait's 1.1 and 19.6 µs, the phase's 2,737.1 µs; with no tick, 6.8 and 37.3 µs, the start wait's 0.8 and 12.3 µs. The Windows item is not measured: it needs a stamp where a frame-critical worker parks and wakes, inside `lactose-task`, whose hooks only its own unit tests compile, so a public surface of `lactose-task` first (D162 addendum at `measure-m2`, round two). `RECORD_RESERVATION` stays one until the quiet window's file is read |

## 2. Third-party claims to re-verify on pinned versions

These were reported by review against upstream sources. Each is re-checked against the exact
version in `Cargo.lock` before the milestone that depends on it.

| Claim | Gates milestone |
|---|---|
| `wide` and glam select fused or unfused multiply-add by target feature. | 0. **Done:** confirmed on wide 1.7.1 (`f32x4::mul_add`) and glam 0.33.12 (`m128_mul_add` in `sse2.rs`, by `target_feature = "fma"`), the one glam the build compiles, through `glamx` 0.3.1; the first reading named glam 0.34.0, which `Cargo.lock` does not hold. The x86-64-v3 pin makes the selection constant |
| `libm`'s x86 runtime detection selects only correctly rounded paths. | 0. **Done:** on 0.2.16 the only run-time detection chooses between FMA3, FMA4 and a soft fused multiply-add, all correctly rounded; the `arch` feature otherwise selects hardware `sqrt`, `fma` and `rint` only. Pinned to `=0.2.16` (D105) |
| Whether Rapier, parry or its math crates call `wide::mul_add` on solver paths. | 1. **Done (`sour-evidence`, a reading of the pinned sources):** no `mul_add` of any kind is reachable from a step. `rapier3d-f64` 0.36.0 (vendored), `parry3d-f64` 0.31.1, `nalgebra` 0.35.0 and `glamx` 0.3.1 contain no `mul_add`, `simd_mul_add`, `MulAdd` or `fma`, so every definition below lacks a caller. `simba` 0.10.2's twenty mentions: trait declarations (`ComplexField::mul_add`, `SimdComplexField::simd_mul_add`); forwarding impls (the blanket `simd_mul_add` to `ComplexField::mul_add`; `WideF64x4::simd_mul_add` to `wide::f64x4::mul_add`, fused under the `fma` target feature and on NEON, compiled because parry's `SimdReal` is `WideF64x4`, and uncalled); `libm::fma`/`fmaf` under `libm_force`, which `enhanced-determinism` sets; unfused `self * a + b` for complex types; per-lane `mul_add`, `libm`'s software `fma` under `libm_force`, for `AutoSimd` types (rapier3d-f64's `SolverBlock`), with no caller on a step; a comment; and the `portable_simd` and `partial_fixed_point_support` modules and the `not(libm_force)` std forms, none compiled. `wide` 1.7.1's polynomials that use `mul_add` (`sin_cos`, `exp`, `ln`, `asin`, `atan2`, `powf`, ...) are not reached: under `libm_force` simba sends `simd_sin`, `simd_cos` and `simd_sin_cos` per lane through `libm` and the other transcendental functions always per lane, forwards to `wide` only `sqrt`, `abs`, `min`, `max`, `blend`, comparisons and reductions besides the uncalled `mul_add`, and parry calls no `wide` function directly. `glam` 0.33.12's `DVec2/3/4::mul_add` (`libm::fma` under its `libm` feature) and `num-traits`' `Float::mul_add` have no caller. `matrixmultiply` 0.3.11 selects FMA kernels at run time but is reached only through nalgebra's dynamic `gemm`, which only multibody constraints use, and the backend inserts impulse joints only. Corroboration, not the answer: the scalar probe's object reading found no fused instruction in the twist contact kernel on aarch64 or x86-64-v3 (`docs/results/scalar-precision-apple-m1-2026-11-14.md`, row 41) |
| Rapier: its determinism feature combines with its parallel solver with bit-identical results for any pool size; SIMD is always on; the 64-bit build has the determinism feature; its math moved to glam-based crates. Whether a single active set makes results depend on unrelated awake bodies is tested by stepping a scene with and without a distant awake body. | 1. **Done:** yes: a body's results depend on every body the world holds or held, not only on the bodies it touches. A 150-body pile, on 1 and 4 threads, first differs at tick 238 beside a 40-ball pile 1 km away, and at tick 274 beside 7 far balls teleported every 25 ticks, keyed above or below the pile; unrelated bodies stepped once and removed change it once new bodies arrive (from tick 40 in the test), because those take the freed arena slots. Bit equality holds only between worlds with identical row histories. Probable carrier, from reading the patched Rapier 0.36.0 (unconfirmed): one awake island for the whole world, with constraint order from world-wide structures (BVH traversal, the contact graph's edge vector, solver-body slots). `crates/physics-rapier/tests/determinism.rs` records all three; the D124 probes (2026-10-11, the backend stepped directly and through `physics-api`'s sync, release, Apple M1) found the dependence confined to large contact graphs and to a solver that has freed its slots: a dynamic capsule on static geometry, one pushing a crate, one bumping a three-crate stack, one pressing two crates against a wall and fifty thrown interacting bodies were bit-equal over 900 to 1,800 ticks with far awake bodies present, arriving at tick 100 and awake throughout, a kinematic capsule's state and query answers likewise, while the 150-body pile differed by 2.7 mm at tick 238 and by 12, 38, 96 and 346 mm at ticks 250, 300, 350 and 400, every body removed and recreated in key order on the same solver differed from a fresh solver by 0.4 mm (a crate), 0.12 m (a stack) and 7.6 m (the pile), and a same-frame in-place reload through the sync, which removes nothing, equalled the untouched twin (3.9e-3 to 1.1e-2 m, a 75-body pile 1.24 to 1.40 m); under the D124 rule (`physics-api` keeps the bytes the backend wrote at setup; a sync whose structural log since its last pass, the pending entries a restore carried included, despawned every body the solver held removes them all, restores those bytes and creates from the rows by a full diff in ascending key order; a sync whose removals leave the solver empty restores the bytes and makes the next sync a full diff) the reload was bit-equal to the fresh process on every path |
| MoltenVK multi-draw-indirect is a CPU loop. Closed by result file `gpu-cost`'s multi-draw figure and a reading of the pinned MoltenVK (D153) | 2. **In part: the reading is done, the record is owed.** The pinned MoltenVK 1.4.2 is tag `v1.4.2` of KhronosGroup/MoltenVK, commit `db66022`, the revision the Vulkan SDK 1.4.363.1's `VERSIONS.txt` names and its `libMoltenVK.dylib` embeds. At that tag (paths under `MoltenVK/MoltenVK/`), `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirect` records one command holding the buffer, offset, count and stride (`Vulkan/vulkan.mm:1631`, `Commands/MVKCmdDraw.mm:989-1025`), and its encode loops over the count on the CPU, finalising the draw state and encoding one Metal `drawIndexedPrimitives:indexType:indexBuffer:indexBufferOffset:indirectBuffer:indirectBufferOffset:` a record, whose arguments the GPU reads at the record's offset (`MVKCmdDraw.mm:1146-1323`, the draw at 1312-1318); `vkCmdDrawIndirect` is encoded the same way (815-971), and nothing in the tag uses a Metal indirect command buffer. Under MoltenVK's defaults, no prefill and synchronous submits (`Utility/MVKEnvironment.h:104-110`), the encode runs inside `vkQueueSubmit` on the submitting thread (`GPUObjects/MVKQueue.mm:78-102`, `Commands/MVKCommandBuffer.mm:304-314`); prefill moves it into recording and asynchronous submits move it onto a dispatch queue, and neither removes the loop. Tessellation, multiview, a triangle fan, 8-bit indices or a shader that reads the draw index add GPU conversion passes, render pass restarts or a binding a record and keep the loop (`MVKCmdDraw.mm:1037-1145`, 1150-1212, 1307-1311); Metal argument buffers and the GPU family do not touch it. The indirect-count draw records nothing (`vulkan.mm:2390-2401`; `drawIndirectCount` is reported false, `GPUObjects/MVKDevice.mm:2833`). `gpu-cost`'s multi-draw takes the plain path: a triangle-list pipeline with no tessellation, no view mask, 32-bit indices, a vertex shader that reads only the vertex index (`shaders/measure/cost.slang`), and MoltenVK's defaults (`tools/xtask` clears `MVK_*` and sets only `MVK_CONFIG_LOG_LEVEL`). The figure reads as that loop: in the lane's fix-round `--all` run on the M1 (branch `measure-m2`, commit 7e3b62a, after fix rounds one and two; not the record) under MoltenVK 1.4.2, 10,000 draws as one multi-draw recorded in 2.4 µs against 269 µs as draws, submitted in 728.1 µs against 666.8 µs, and the pass took 1,798.8 µs of GPU time against 1,670.9 µs (medians; the pass with its clear alone 377.7 µs, submitted in 51.0 µs). The multi-draw saves the recording of 10,000 commands and keeps their encode: about 68 ns of the submit a record against 62 ns a draw, and about 0.14 µs of GPU time a record against 0.13 µs. The record is the quiet window's `gpu-cost` file under MoltenVK; with its multi-draw figure the row is Done |
| Slang's Metal target supports argument buffers, function constants and framebuffer fetch. Shown first by the native Metal spike's shaders, which are MSL the Slang compiler emitted, then held by a pipeline state made from each cooked shader: on the M1 from the cooked MSL, on the macos-arm64 runner from the metallib (D153) | 2. **Done.** **The spike's shaders** (`docs/results/spike-metal-apple-m1-2026-11-17.md`): the SDK's Slang 2026.17-25 emitted MSL with an argument buffer of 1024 textures indexed non-uniformly, two function constants and a framebuffer-fetch input; Metal compiled it as MSL 3.1 with no error (Metal reports no warnings at run time; offline `metal` warns twice on the frame's vertex functions) and every pipeline read back exact. A function with constants must be specialised even where each has a default. **The cooked half, on the M1** (branch `slang`): the engine shader, `shaders/surface.slang`, cooked by the pinned Slang 2026.20 with a fixed array of 1024 images and one of 64 samplers in an argument buffer at `[[buffer(1)]]`, indexed non-uniformly, a function constant and a framebuffer-fetch input, made four pipeline states, two from the MSL compiled through Metal at run time as MSL 3.1 and two from the metallibs `metal` compiled for macOS 14, each specialised, made and not run, under Metal API validation and with shader validation loaded (each validator's canary heard), and no other message (`cargo xtask gpu --driver system`, Apple M1 with argument buffers tier 2 and Apple family 7, macOS 27.2.1, Xcode 27.2; run again after `rhi-null`'s merge, the cook writing each shader through `CookedShader::encode`). **The runner's half** (CI 44300942552 at `a3f7fe5`, the `shaders` jobs' first green run): `shaders (macos-latest)`, job 134790188291 (macOS 26.7.2, Xcode 26.6, an Apple Paravirtual device with argument buffers tier 2 and Apple family 5), cooked the engine shader and made its four pipeline states under Metal's validation, two from the MSL and two from the metallibs, its log reading `msl surface vs_main+fs_main ok`, `msl surface vs_main+fs_resolve ok`, `metallib surface vs_main+fs_main ok`, `metallib surface vs_main+fs_resolve ok`, `pipelines: 4` and `gpu: ok`; `shaders (oldest macOS)`, job 134792779325 (macOS 15.8.2, Xcode 16.4), made the same four from the shaders that job cooked and printed the same lines |
| Apple M1 lacks 64-bit atomics. | 3 |
| glam or its fork has a feature (such as fast-math) that changes numerics on the pinned version; if so it goes on the cargo-deny denylist. | 0. **Done:** `fast-math` changes numerics on glam 0.33.0 to 0.33.7, is a no-op from 0.33.8 and gone in 0.34; `scalar-math`, `core-simd`, `libm` and `nostd-libm` select different code. `deny.toml` bans `fast-math` and `core-simd` for glam and says why the other three are not denied. Which version Rapier selects, the milestone 1 check, is closed: glam 0.33.12, through `glamx` 0.3.1, is the one glam the build compiles (`Cargo.lock`), and `fast-math` is a no-op there |
| `bytemuck_derive` on the pinned version: support for data-carrying enums under `NoUninit` and `CheckedBitPattern`, and whether a `PodInOption` derive exists for `NetId`. | 0. **Done:** 1.12.1 derives both traits for data-carrying enums with an explicit repr; it has no `PodInOption` derive, so `NetId` keeps its hand-written impls in `lactose-core`. `[T; N]` is plain data only for `T: Pod`, hence `lactose_core::PlainArray`. Its struct `CheckedBitPattern` derive needs `bytemuck` as a direct dependency and accepts `r#repr`, so `#[component]` writes and checks that impl itself (D97) |
| loom's current limitations (SeqCst modelled as AcqRel, no load buffering) and feasible preemption bound for the latch and sleep model. | 0. **Done:** confirmed on loom 0.7.2; the sleep protocol uses explicit `fence(SeqCst)` (D110). Eleven of the fourteen models are exhaustive and three are bounded (two at 5 preemptions, the idle-release model at 4); all fourteen run in about 50 s in release. Paths the models leave uncovered: the keep decision itself (`keep_woken_task`, `fire_timers`, `submit_task`; the model drives the shipped sleep and gate functions with a stand-in waker), a thief stealing a kept entry between its push and pop, shutdown with a kept entry, the real deque. Lane `interp-loom` (2026-11-18): loom 0.7.2 places a backtracking point only against an atomic's last access, so its search is not complete within a bound and depends on the threads' shape: a writer that loads a word before it stores it hides a reader's earlier load of it. net-host's ring-wrap model with the reader on the main thread runs one execution and passes with each of its four orderings weakened; with the writer on the main thread it runs 242,926 and each weakening fails. A loom model's pass is evidence only through mutants that fail it |
| `tracing-tracy` per-span overhead, to confirm per-system and per-range spans fit the budget. | 0. **Done:** on an M1, a `tracing` span costs about 1 ns with no subscriber and 8 ns with one; tracing-tracy 0.11.4 costs about 165 ns per span with no server connected. Per-system and per-stolen-range spans fit; per-chunk spans would not |
| Rapier's serialised snapshot is iteration-order stable enough to hash. | 1. **Done:** the hash of the backend's bytes after the conformance run (`lactose-physics-rapier` `the_conformance_run`, tick `TICKS + 1`) is recorded under SOUR and equal under workers 1 and 4, a second run of the same configuration, layouts rows-1/7/64 and bytes-16384, chaos seeds 1 to 3, and across linux-x64, windows-x64 and macos-arm64 in nightly 43703297881 at `c50b688` |
| Rapier's 64-bit build uses SIMD after its glam migration, which decides the 64-bit cost argument. | 1. **Done:** yes for the contact solver (`docs/results/scalar-precision-apple-m1-2026-11-14.md`). No feature gates it: parry3d-f64 0.31.1 makes `SimdReal` `simba::simd::WideF64x4` (`wide::f64x4`) for f64 unconditionally, and `simd8` cannot be combined with `enhanced-determinism`. The staged island solver solves every rigid-body contact as a 4-lane chunk, an ungroupable one as a padded 1-lane chunk. In a release build of `lactose-physics-rapier` at `4c2cef5`, the kernel the default friction model selects, `ContactWithTwistFriction<WideF64x4>::solve`, is 832 NEON `.2d` instructions and no scalar arithmetic on aarch64; on x86-64-v3 (cross-compiled, not run) the solve-pass closure it is inlined into holds 502 `ymm` instructions and one scalar division, none fused. glam 0.33.12's `DVec3` and `DQuat` have no SIMD backend on any target, and `enhanced-determinism` turns on `glamx/scalar-math`; `RigidBodyVelocity<f64>::integrate`, which a step calls only through CCD, is scalar on aarch64 and mostly scalar on x86-64-v3; the substep integration, inlined into the solver's worker, was not isolated. The f64 contact solver runs two lanes per NEON instruction against f32's four; the glam-based code examined is scalar f64. The cost half of the scalar decision (D146), with the 64-bit loop as measured on the quiet M1 beside it: a re-simulated tick of the spike's thrown scene costs 387.7 µs at `c50b688` (`docs/results/rollback-seven-ticks-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`), and the physics phase at 2,000 awake bodies 1,954.3 µs against 3 ms at `bef3455` (`docs/results/physics-phase-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`). No 32-bit loop was timed and none is owed: section 16's rule does not weigh cost |
| `std::fs::File::sync_all` issues `F_FULLFSYNC` on Apple platforms and `FlushFileBuffers` on Windows, and `std::fs::rename` on Windows replaces an existing file atomically, on the pinned toolchain; and the replaced file's durability after a power loss on each platform (the `Io::replace` contract, D130). Apple part confirmed by the D130 review: std 1.99.0 issues `F_FULLFSYNC` for `sync_all`, and a directory sync with it works on APFS and HFS+. The replace's order (write, sync of the temporary, rename, directory sync) is asserted through the pool by `crates/io/src/tests.rs` (on Windows, which has no directory sync, the last step asserted is the rename), and a rename over a path the process itself holds open by `crates/io/tests/requests.rs`. Both pass on Windows: CI's check (windows-latest) ran all 52 of `lactose-io`'s tests and passed them at the io implementation's merge (run 40110591871 at `c78afdd`) and at `ca65ec9` (run 43577819272), `tests::a_replace_writes_and_syncs_its_temporary_then_renames_then_syncs_the_directory` and `requests::a_file_opened_before_a_replace_reads_the_previous_content_after_it` among them. Windows part read by the `data-path` lane in the pinned std (1.99.0, `library/std/src`): `sync_all` (`fs.rs:774`) is `FlushFileBuffers` (`sys/fs/windows.rs:400-403`); `rename` (`fs.rs:2892`, `sys/fs/mod.rs:75`, `sys/fs/windows.rs:1321`) is `MoveFileExW(old, new, MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING)` (line 1322) without `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, falling back only on `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` (line 1327) to `SetFileInformationByHandle` with `FileRenameInfoEx` and `FILE_RENAME_FLAG_REPLACE_IF_EXISTS | FILE_RENAME_FLAG_POSIX_SEMANTICS` (lines 1355-1372); std's default share mode includes `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` (line 206). Microsoft's `MoveFileExW` page says the flag replaces the existing file's contents and does not say whether the replace is atomic; its `FlushFileBuffers` page says the call writes the file's buffered data to the device and says nothing of the device's own cache; its page on alternatives to TxF names `ReplaceFile` as one method for replacing a document with a new file, without calling it atomic. **Done** as a reading: the calls are the ones `Io::replace` describes. That the replace is atomic on Windows is relied on and not documented, and the replaced file's durability after a power loss is each platform's contract; neither is verified (D139) | 1 |
| Directory fsync after a rename (D130): what `sync_all` on a directory returns on each platform and file system the game may save to (`EINVAL` on Linux FUSE and vboxsf, `ENOTSUP` from `F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS exFAT and SMB, nothing on Windows, where `io` does not sync the directory), and whether `Io::replace` should treat such a result as success rather than `Io` with the new content in place and its durability unknown. Apple measured (lane io-impl, M1, macOS 14.7.4, load averages 6.99 7.94 9.22): `sync_all` on a directory returns `Ok` on APFS (data volume and a disk image), HFS+ and exFAT (fskit) disk images, and `Io::replace` into each resolves `Ok`; SMB not measured. Linux (FUSE, vboxsf) and Windows not yet measured. Until a failure is seen, `Io::replace` keeps reporting a failing directory sync as `Io` with the new content in place; the pool's test hook shows the directory sync runs after the rename. Re-gated to milestone 4 (D139): the file systems still unmeasured are deployment targets, and architecture section 22.1 gives the disk-full and corrupt-save paths to milestone 4 | 4 |

## 3. Console assumptions to keep out of milestone 0 interfaces

| Assumption | Why it will hurt | Mitigation now |
|---|---|---|
| Software zstd and LZ4 in `io` and the pack format | PlayStation and Xbox have hardware decompression | `io` exposes a read-with-decompress request, not a read followed by a decompress call |
| std threads, `park`, QoS and sysctl core queries | Consoles need fixed affinity | Worker creation goes through a platform trait in `task` |
| Writable disk for pipeline caches and crash dumps | Not guaranteed | Both paths are optional and report failure as a value |
| Slang has no public PSSL target | PlayStation shaders | The cooker's backend list is a table, not code |
| No allocator decision | Milestone 3 budgets need tagging from the start | Tagged `GlobalAlloc` in `core` from milestone 0 |

## 4. Open items

| Item | Gates milestone |
|---|---|
| Player portability between hosts | 4 (decided-later table, architecture section 22.1) |
| Multi-process server sharding: out of scope, kept possible by region ownership and command-based cross-region interaction (architecture section 9.6) | none |
| Local x86-64 runs on Apple Silicon: Rosetta on macOS 14 raises an illegal-instruction fault on the x86-64-v3 baseline, so x86-64 tests and SOUR architecture mode run on CI runners and the x86 desktop, never on the Mac. Re-check after a macOS upgrade. The Mac moves to macOS 27.2.1 on 17 November 2026 (D154): the re-check is one boundary crate's tests, built for `x86_64-apple-darwin` and run under Rosetta there, in the coordinator's pass after the upgrade | none |
| Windows efficiency-core steering: on Windows `ThreadClass::Background` sets thread priority and then the thread's power-throttling state, with `ControlMask` and `StateMask` both `THREAD_POWER_THROTTLING_EXECUTION_SPEED`, which Windows 11 calls EcoQoS and documents as the most efficient frequency and the efficiency cores, on mains power as on battery (Windows 10 applies it as its low QoS, on battery only). Every other class, `Io` included, sets both masks to 0 and hands the thread back to the system's management, so on Windows, unlike Linux, a later `enter` of another class undoes `Background`'s; no class opts out of throttling (D17's addendum). Re-gated to milestone 2 (D138): the first Windows client is where background work runs beside frame work, and the measurement needs hybrid x86 hardware. Lane `eco-qos` (D153): measured on the laptop, a hybrid part, by the WHISK benchmark's rows with the two background zstd tasks, before and after, and on the desktop if it is one. **Built** (lane `eco-qos`): a Windows unit test in `lactose-core`, in the `check (windows-latest, base)` job, enters each other class, then `Background`, then the class again on one thread, and holds that every set call succeeds. It reads the masks back through `NtQueryInformationThread` and the kernel's class 49, and asserts them exactly where the kernel answers; a refused read is printed and fails nothing, and then the test proves no mask. Whether CI's Windows answers the read or refuses it is not recorded, since nextest hides a passing test's output. The rules for what `enter` returns (priority first, both calls always made, a reset refused as an unknown class counted as success, each message naming its call and the system's code) are unit-tested on every host. Open until the owner's runs on the laptop are read: before, after and the before again, on mains power in one recorded power mode. Each zstd row is read against its own quiet row and against the two before runs' spread, since on the laptop the zstd load made the frame workers faster; the quiet rows must not move beyond the spread. What EcoQoS costs the zstd tasks' throughput is not measured: the benchmark prints none | 2 |
| `lld-link` on Windows (architecture section 18) is configured (D125): `.cargo/config.toml` sets `linker = "rust-lld"` for x86_64-pc-windows-msvc, from the pinned toolchain's sysroot, with no install step. Linux links with rustc's bundled lld, the default for x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu since 1.90. mold is not taken. The edit-to-playing measurement is `cargo xtask measure edit-to-playing`. Quiet window (`docs/results/edit-to-playing-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`, load averages 3.79 7.12 5.87 before and 8.10 7.89 6.19 after, the task benchmark's and the measurement's own builds): slower median 1.56 s (sketch 1.51 s, app function 1.56 s) against 30 s, so criterion 9 is met on macos-arm64. The `measure` workflow's run 43659357193 at `bef3455` gave 3.21 s on a linux-x64 runner (sketch 0.93 s) and 5.83 s on a windows-x64 runner (sketch 1.89 s), each on one frame worker; a runner's file is checked in for an operating system the desktop does not boot (`docs/plans/milestone-1.md`, decision 5.9). The desktop boots Windows and another system (D144), so only linux-x64 can need one. That run's linux-x64 file is checked in as a runner's, `docs/results/edit-to-playing-github-runner-linux-x64-2026-11-15.md`: it is criterion 9's on Linux unless session B runs there, and then session B's file is (D149). Milestone 1's part is **Done** (D151): criterion 9 is met at the exit on the M1's file. Its Windows figure, and its Linux figure if the desktop boots Linux (D149), are session B's, in milestone 2's desktop sitting (the row "The x86 half of milestones 0 and 1 (D151)"). Until then Windows has the runner's 5.83 s above and the labelled laptop's 3.62 s (`docs/results/edit-to-playing-laptop-windows-2026-11-15.md`), which prove no criterion | 2 |
| The first CI runs after the lld-link change (D125). Attempt 2 restored with a full match, printed "Cache up-to-date." and passed in 729 s (889 s before lld-link), its build 9m49s against 9m56s, so lld-link's saving is within noise; the vctip line did not appear. The rust-cache post step logs harmless ENOENT errors for `target/tmp/vendor-unsafe`, the scratch workspace of `xtask vendor`. **Done**, the trend over the 36 runs after it whose Windows jobs passed, 38137027647 at `f4cea8c` (11 October) to 43577819272 at `ca65ec9` (14 November): check (windows-latest) took 4.2 to 12.5 min, median 7.4, against the estimate of about 12.6 min warm, and windows-lint-release, shown as "clippy and release tests (windows-latest)", 1.9 to 5.5 min, median 3.3, against 3.5 to 4. The 12.5 min was the first of them. From 17 October check took 4.2 to 9.4 min in every run but 43532914184 at `6945aed`, which found no cache and took 11.1 min (windows-lint-release 5.5). Neither job reached its 15-minute limit. The three runs of 17 October that failed before any step ran are left out. The "Terminate orphan process ... (vctip)" line no longer appears in warm runs; it was in every Windows job that reached its test build since 5 Oct, and a cold run may still show it from zstd-sys's C build: the cold run 43532914184 printed it once in check (windows-latest), the warm run at `ca65ec9` did not | 1 |
| A region's asset set beyond its baseline's (architecture section 14, D140). The rule is stated: the baseline's set, the `AssetRef`s of resident entities the baseline does not hold, and a spawnable set the baseline declares; a spawn that names an asset outside the table is a tick-stamped asset failure. At milestone 1 the set is `Baseline::assets` and nothing spawns or migrates an entity that names an asset outside it. Design with streaming: how `persist` reads the residents' `AssetRef`s out of the region's section, where the baseline declares its spawnable set (a `lactose-bundle` format change), how a region's table is resolved again when a resident migrates in, and how the failure reaches both ends at one tick | 3 |

| Wake latency against the 20 µs P99 criterion. **Spike done** (`docs/results/spike-wake-apple-m1-2026-10-06.md`): at a known tick deadline a stepped timed park reaches P99 6 to 10 µs for 0.6% of a core, and the shipped keeper is being fixed, since macOS timer coalescing made it 5.5 ms late at 60 Hz. From an unpredictable push, no primitive, QoS class or real-time policy gets under 20 µs on the M1 (best P99 24 to 29 µs): the floor is the cores leaving deep idle, removable only by a thread waking every 150 µs or less (P99 6 to 8.5 µs at 1.3 to 1.9% of a core, power cost unmeasured). Decided (D113): tick start under 20 µs (35 µs since D138's restatement, on macOS 50 µs since D156), push from an idle pool under 35 µs, no warm sleeper; x86 desktop numbers still to come. The quiet window (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`): push from an idle pool P50 13.3 µs, P99 31.6 µs, under D113's 35 µs. A labelled data point that is not the desktop (`docs/results/whisk-laptop-windows-2026-11-15.md`, an i7-12650H laptop with 6 performance and 4 efficiency cores on Windows 11 at `bef3455`, about a quarter of its processor busy with other work): a push to a parked pool takes P50 145.9 µs, P99 236.4 µs, max 828.6 µs. Its makespan fails mostly with no background load (P99 4.02 for wide-shallow, 6.58 for fan-in-fan-out) and does better with two zstd tasks running, which fits workers waking slowly from idle. It is not the timed wait (D144): the pool of the wake phase parks with no deadline, std's `park`, which is `WaitOnAddress` with no timeout, and is woken by `WakeByAddressSingle`. The makespan phase's 1 ms gap between runs is a `sleep_until` through the keeper and lasted a tick on Windows, so its runs began from a pool idle about sixteen times longer than a frame's gap; lane `timer-windows` runs the benchmark on the laptop before and after, which shows that part. The rest is not diagnosed. The candidates are the cores' idle states, the scheduler's choice of core on a hybrid part, Windows' power throttling of a process without a foreground window, and the system timer's rate; the laptop probe briefed in `docs/plans/windows-timer-proposal.md`, section 5, part A, splits them. A decision comes due if the desktop's Windows file fails the wake or the makespan. The M1's half is **Done** (D151): the quiet window's P99 is 31.6 µs, under 35. The x86 half is session A's, in milestone 2's desktop sitting, and the laptop's miss is part of the finding it settles (the row "The x86 half of milestones 0 and 1 (D151)") | 2 (carried, D138 and D151) |
| The executor benchmark runs under the `TASK_UNSPECIFIED` task role from a terminal, where user-interactive QoS is clamped to priority 31. **Done:** it sets `TASK_FOREGROUND_APPLICATION` on macOS and prints the role it ran in, since lane `measure-role` through `lactose_task::take_foreground_task_role`, which records beside the role the priority a user-interactive thread is then granted: the role read back is the request, which macOS accepts however the process was started, and the priority is what was granted. On the M1, before the request and then after it: 31 then 47 from a terminal; 37 then 37 for a launchd job of `ProcessType` `Interactive`; 20 then 20 for one with no `ProcessType` or `Standard`, and under `taskpolicy -c utility`; 4 then 4 for `Background` or `Adaptive`, and under `taskpolicy -b`; a child inherits its parent's. The role cannot be given back: on the M1 under macOS 27.2 a request for `TASK_UNSPECIFIED` returns `KERN_INVALID_ARGUMENT` and the role stays, and `TASK_DEFAULT_APPLICATION` puts a user-interactive thread at priority 46, so a case that needs the unspecified role runs in a process of its own | 0 |

| Tick-start latency against the P99 criterion: 20 µs (35 µs since D138's restatement, on macOS 50 µs since D156). After D116 (the keeper runs the task it fires, injector guard) interleaved A/B runs at 60 Hz give P50 3 to 5 µs and P99 23 to 30 µs against P99 41 to 44 µs before, with 0 or 1 of 3,000 deadlines over 1 ms; the reviewer measured the same build at P99 5.7 µs in one state of this machine and 22 to 24 µs in another at similar load, cause unknown. A run on the quiet Mac (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-10-10.md`, load 1.9 to 2.1, nothing else running) gives P50 2.8 µs, P99 29.9 µs, max 74.8 µs, none over 1 ms: the quiet state is not the fast state, and the cause of the two states is still unknown. Not demonstrated; the x86 desktop numbers are next. The full benchmark on the merged tree (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-10-09.md`, UTC date, commit `00f1f3d`) measures P50 3.7 µs, P99 24.5 µs, max 46.3 µs, none of 5,000 over 1 ms, at load 3.7 falling to 1.7. Known remaining costs: when other timers are pending the keeper still unparks a sleeper to take over the watch before it runs the kept task, and each re-registration wakes a sleeper to become the keeper. The quiet window (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`, `bef3455`, load averages 1.51 2.07 3.66 before and 4.04 7.23 5.90 after, the benchmark's own): at 60 Hz P50 2.6 µs, P99 27.0 µs, max 69.6 µs, none of 5,000 over 1 ms. Not met, as on 9 and 10 October; D138's one attempt and its restatement at 35 µs stand. On Windows the timed wait is far off: the laptop (`docs/results/whisk-laptop-windows-2026-11-15.md`, 15 to 19% of the processor busy during these phases) gives, at a 2 ms period, P50 14.9 ms, P99 15.5 ms and 4,998 of 5,000 deadlines over 1 ms, and at 16.667 ms, P50 4.3 ms and P99 15.9 ms. That is Windows' default timer tick, 15.6 ms, and the cause is confirmed (D144, `docs/plans/windows-timer-proposal.md`). In std 1.99.0, `park_timeout` on Windows is `WaitOnAddress` with its timeout rounded up to whole milliseconds (`library/std/src/sys/sync/futex/windows.rs`; `dur2timeout` in `sys/pal/windows/mod.rs`), which ends on the tick, and so do `Condvar::wait_timeout` and the channels' `recv_timeout`; `std::thread::sleep` is a high-resolution waitable timer that no waker can end. The laptop's two probes (1,000 samples a row, each row twice, 10 to 46% of the processor busy with other work): a `park_timeout` of 0.5 to 10 ms lasts about 16 ms whatever is asked, and the high-resolution timer overshoots by about half a millisecond at the median whatever is asked. Under the stepped wait of `Sleep::park` to a fixed 60 Hz schedule with the 50 µs spin, a deadline is passed by P50 7.9 ms and P99 15.7 to 15.8 ms as shipped, by P50 0.78 to 0.81 ms and P99 2.0 ms with `timeBeginPeriod(1)` held, and by P50 0.30 ms and P99 1.0 to 1.1 ms with the timer in place of the park. With the timer and a spin threshold of 500 µs: P50 under a microsecond, P99 0.49 to 0.59 ms, 0.77 to 1.11% of one core. With 2,000 µs: 6.9 to 7.7% of a core, and 56 to 103 deadlines of 1,000 still over 35 µs late. Only a keeper that never waited was under 20 µs at P99. Decided (D144): on Windows the keeper's step is a wait on a high-resolution waitable timer and a wake event, it spins the last 500 µs, and tick start there is held to 1 ms at P99; lane `timer-windows` builds it (the row "The keeper's timed wait on Windows (D144)"). The quiet window's P99 at 60 Hz is 27.0 µs on the M1 (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`). `keeper`'s one attempt ran on the M1 on 16 November and did not reach 20 µs (D138's addendum). The keeper's final timed wait is not what is late: 43 of 3,020 park exits came from a wait that passed the deadline, and the P99 of the park's exit lateness over all its exits is 4.6 µs. Timing probes place the lost time after the park, in one stall of about 13 to 28 µs on the keeper's first access past its L1 cache (not confirmed by hardware counters). A 200 µs spin that reads a 256 KiB buffer gave P99 18.25 to 21.04 µs against 23.29 to 24.38 µs as shipped in two interleaved A/B blocks of the benchmark (load averages 6.44 and 3.72 at their starts, 1.19 to 3.73 between and after their runs), for 0.25% of a core; a run of it with no base beside it gave 17.96 µs (load 2.64 falling to 2.41, in a block whose load later rose to 21.8). That is not D116's fast state (under 6 µs), so what separates the two states is not found. The M1's half is **Done** by D138's restatement: tick start is held to 35 µs at P99 on macOS and Linux, and the quiet window at the merge (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-16.md`, `0ec2d90`, load averages 5.71 6.50 6.70 before and 1.97 4.51 5.75 after, the benchmark's own) gives P99 24.4 µs at 60 Hz against it, none of 5,000 over 1 ms. The earlier quiet window's 27.0 µs (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-15.md`, whose verdict was written against 20 µs) and every run of the shipped benchmark in the attempt (P99 22.21 to 24.71 µs) are under it too. On macOS 27.2.1 the M1's file at `a539d60` gives 36.2 µs and fails it (`docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-17.md`); the files of macOS 14.7.4 stay milestone 1's proof, and the finding is the row "Tick start on macOS 27.2.1 (D154)". On macOS the criterion is 50 µs since D156. The x86 half is session A's, in milestone 2's desktop sitting (the row "The x86 half of milestones 0 and 1 (D151)") | 2 (carried, D138 and D151) |

| The further gaps of the `lactose-physics-api` (D119) row (lane xtask-policy, review round 2: `nm -u` on the rapier3d-f64 and parry3d-f64 rlibs and the call graph of `lactose-physics-rapier`'s test binaries): Rapier and parry call the platform library past `libm_force` at sites no boundary lint can reach: glamx's 3x3 symmetric eigen decomposition (`acos`, `cos`), from parry's `MassProperties::with_inertia_matrix` (the sum of two nonzero mass properties calls it, a body with two colliders or a collider and additional mass, and so do compound, convex-polyhedron, trimesh and voxel mass properties); `Bvh::optimize_incremental`'s `log2`, in every step's broad phase; `GenericJointConstraint::lock_axes`'s `sin`, for the target of an angular motor on a joint that touches a multibody link (`motor_angular_generic`, inlined; not for locked or limited axes, as first written); trimesh pseudo-normals' `acos`; and the soft-body solver's `exp`, `pow` and `acos`, linked into the step though no scene uses a soft body. `RevoluteJoint::angle` (`asin`) and parry's `VectorExt::angle` (`acos`) are linked by no test today. Traced (`docs/plans/physics-evidence-proposal.md`, section 1; aarch64-apple-darwin at `b2e2a41`, the seven maths functions the test binaries import from the platform library interposed and every call counted with its calling frames): the nine recording tests that step Rapier, the four of `lactose-physics-rapier`, the spike's four and the headless run, reach `log2` and no other from a step, 2 to 4,658 calls each, all from `optimization_config` under `Bvh::optimize_incremental`, `BroadPhaseBvh::update` or `take_deferred_optimize`, and `PhysicsPipeline::detect_collisions`; the dynamic capsule's `atan2` and `fmod` are the spike's own yaw measurement, outside the step; 50 other tests of the backend reach `log2` alone. **Done** (D145), site by site. `log2`: accepted. Its two values decide one rounded count, `target_optimized_subtree_count`; for every leaf count from 1 to 20,000,000 the rounded expression stays 2.4e-7 or more from a boundary and one ulp of `log2` moves it by less than 9.6e-17, so the count is the same for any `log2` within 2.5e9 ulp; a parry bump that changes `optimization_config` reopens it. The eigen decomposition: accepted for milestone 1 and gated to milestone 3 (the row "glamx's symmetric eigen decomposition calls the platform library"). The backend gives a body one collider or a unit ball's mass, never both, and builds a ball, a capsule or a cuboid; a `RigidBody` row that gains an offset or tilted `Collider` reaches it once (`attach_collider` before `Rapier::update` clears the additional mass), and the same call rebuilds the mass from zero: with `acos` and `cos` scaled by 1 + 1e-6 the scene's bytes after 180 ticks are unchanged. The motor's `sin`, the soft-body solver, the pseudo-normals and the two `angle` functions: accepted as unreachable. The backend inserts impulse joints without motors and no soft body, a snapshot that holds a multibody joint or a soft body is refused, a write and a copy refuse a shape the backend did not build, and among boundary crates only `lactose-physics-rapier` may name `rapier3d-f64`. The trace is not a gate: it runs by hand on macOS. | Float `%` inside the boundary, on a scalar and through glam's `Rem` (`DVec3 % DVec3` is `self.x.rem(rhs.x)` per lane), calls the platform `fmod`, and clippy's `disallowed-methods` does not see operators, so no boundary lint stops it (found by the review of lane `xtask-policy`, `nm -u` on aarch64-apple-darwin and x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu). `fmod`'s result is exact, so a conforming library returns the same bits. For the planner, one of: accept `%` on that ground; measure it across the three CI targets and restate the reason for section 8.2's `rem_euclid`/`div_euclid` bans, which today cite the platform `fmod`; or route `%` through `math` and refuse it in boundary crates. **Decided (D149): the third, and its first half is on main.** A float remainder inside the boundary is `Scalar`'s `%` or `Scalar::rem_euclid`, the `libm` crate's `fmod` (`crates/math/src/scalar.rs`), with 361 golden cases each that CI holds on linux-x64 and windows-x64 (the row "Cross-architecture agreement of `lactose-math`"). `%` and `%=` on `f32` and `f64` and the `Rem` and `RemAssign` of glam's vectors and simba's SIMD types are banned in boundary crates (architecture section 8.2), so the Euclidean bans keep their reason and nothing is measured on the CI targets. At `6f0508a` every `%` in the boundary crates' sources and tests is an integer remainder, a `tracing` sigil or `Scalar`'s own, the vendored Rapier's are integer remainders, and the trace of D145 saw no `fmod` from a step. Owed with architecture mode's move to every pull request: `clippy::modulo_arithmetic` denied in every boundary crate and held there by `cargo xtask boundary`, with fixtures in `tools/xtask/tests/boundary_lints.rs`. On the pinned 1.99.0 that lint refuses `%` and `%=` on a float value, a constant divisor included, and on signed integers, and passes unsigned ones and two constants of one sign, which the compiler folds; it does not see a float `%` whose left operand is a reference (`&f64 % f64`, which a closure over `iter()` computes), `Rem::rem` or `rem_assign` called as a function, a generic `T: Rem` used at a float, or the `Rem` and `RemAssign` of glam's vectors and simba's `AutoSimd` (probes outside the tree). `disallowed-methods` on `core::ops::Rem::rem` and `core::ops::RemAssign::rem_assign` sees the calls, and `lactose-physics-rapier`, the one boundary crate that can name glam or simba, gets a check of its `src` for the operator; the reference and generic forms, and the operator in that crate's tests, stay with review. A review holds the whole ban until then | 4 |

The spike does not time a gather, and no criterion or budget of milestone 1 reads its time; the figure above was taken at `eb5cf22`, before the ECS hot-path lanes (D137), and steers. The rings the bound admits are recorded as the bytes their slots would write (`<scene>.<clock>.ring.*`: 16.9 MB for sixty slots of the thrown scene, 11.1 MB of it the backend's), and what a ring holds as copies is read when `app` sizes it (the row "The frame re-simulation budget has no figure"). The presentation check is `lactose-presentation`'s test (D153); the visual blend of a correction is milestone 4's. **The presentation check is done** (branch `presentation`, `crates/presentation/tests/interpolation.rs`): a prediction world's body, despawned and spawned again under its key at x 10 from x 0 between two published ticks: without `Teleported` the pair holds it in both snapshots unflagged, and the frame shows x 5 at fraction 0.5 with the camera's pivot there; with `Teleported` at the newer snapshot's tick the pair is flagged and every fraction shows x 10; one at the older snapshot's tick blends. The frame's fraction is the weight over the whole pair, as the renderer draws, and the camera's pivot is where the renderer's rule draws the mesh it follows over pairs one, two and five ticks apart | 2. **Done** |
