| Region resources mutated after relevance are never corrected in the prediction world. `OwnedBy` is not replicated, so a client's edges are as old as each region's document and no prediction system may name it; a client's hooks and `lactose-predict`'s corrections follow no edge (D141: each tombstone and each move applied to the one entity named, `World::despawn_alone` and `World::move_alone_to_region`, which a `Server` world refuses). Open, decided together before a game predicts an owner's despawn or region move: (1) a prediction system's own `Commands::despawn` or `Commands::move_to_region` of a claimed owner cascades at apply through the prediction world's edges, a document's and its own predicted ones, so it takes a proxy the server released after the document (gone from the prediction world, or in a region the frames never placed it in, until a whole send or a full frame; the replicated world keeps it) and misses an entity the server gave the owner since; a tombstone of the owner does not end it, since an owner that returns (a re-entry spawn, which carries its origin key, or a region's next document) owns again what the document's stale edge says, for a later cascade to take, whatever the ring's depth, and only a bound owner spawned again under its bare `Net` key, which no hook does, is found again only inside the ring window, while its alias lives; the one candidate known is the ECS's, that in a prediction world the cascade stops at an entity the local player has not claimed, which is a switch on the world's kind at command apply and would reverse, for the predicted path, D141's rule that `Commands::despawn` means one thing in both worlds (no candidate without a switch is known: stopping at unclaimed entities in every kind would change what a server's despawn takes); (2) a replicated `OwnedBy`, for a game that reads ownership on a client: its row can arrive before the removal that makes it legal, so `insert_erased` refuses it under D133's forest and a claimed entity's correction is refused at every frame until a rule for an out-of-order edge exists; it moves the protocol hash and its pins; and an exact row carries the predicted origin where a document's owner is stored under its `Net` key until the client learns the owner's bind (D142 gives a server every bind of a save before its first load and leaves a client's to the stream and to each document's rows; a replicated `OwnedBy` either sends the binds of a document's owners beside the relevance message or lets the row correct them); (3) an owned entity that a region document placed and whose handle the client does not hold stays after its owner's tombstone until the region unloads or a full frame, the open item of `lactose-net-host`'s "Interest and the packer", which the cascade used to hide when the edge happened to be fresh | 4 |
| Interpolation slot footprint (about 530 bytes; 5k entities about 2.6 MB) against the remote-upload budget, the reader's retry rate under one writer at 30 Hz with the stated orderings, and a loom model of the sequence lock. The reader's retry rate under a 30 Hz writer and the footprint are in result file `remote-upload-5k` (D153). The rate follows the cores the reader and the writer run on, from 8.6e-5 unpaced to 1.9e-2 paced on slow cores in the probes of `measure-m2`'s review, so the file gives it with the share of frames on each class of core beside it, and no figure of it is a criterion (D164). The loom model: **Done** (lane `interp-loom`, head 09449ad): the slot's lock is `crates/net-host/src/seqlock.rs`, generic over a `Memory` trait and the ring's shape; the buffer runs it over the standard atomics at eight states of eight words, and `src/models.rs` (feature `loom`) runs the same `reset`, `push` and `read` over loom's atomics at two states of two words, no two pushed states sharing a word. Three models, each a writer on the main thread against one spawned reader reading twice, at preemption bound 2 (bounded, not exhaustive), every read equal to the slot's contents after one write and none older than the read before it: `the_ring_wraps_under_a_reader` (a full ring pushed twice past its oldest; 242,926 executions), `the_generation_changes_under_a_reader` (a release, a reallocation and a push; 139,452) and `the_ring_grows_at_its_front_under_a_reader` (a push at the ring's front, then one past it; 129,052), each made to show one kind of mixed read: state words, `generation`, and `count` against the states. At bound 3 all three pass too (1,246,450, 735,412 and 606,050 executions). The lock's four orderings stronger than `Relaxed`, each weakened in turn, make all three models find a torn read: the writer's `fence(Release)` (`*_without_the_writers_release_fence`), the even sequence's `Release` (`*_without_the_even_sequences_release`), the reader's first `Acquire` (`*_without_the_readers_first_acquire`) and its `fence(Acquire)` (`*_without_the_readers_acquire_fence`); none is stronger than it needs to be, and `seqlock.rs` states which release pairs with which acquire. `count` stored after the even sequence store (mutant P10 of the concurrency review) passes the first two models, the hardware stress test and Miri's, and fails the third at once. A unit test without loom (`each_ordering_stronger_than_relaxed_has_one_site`) holds each ordering to one site, which is how the weakened models relax one at a time: it counts the calls one thread makes and the sites in the code, so a second site on a reader's retry path fails it too. Residuals: loom 0.7.2 backtracks only against an atomic's last access, so the writer is the main thread: with the reader on the main thread the ring-wrap model runs one execution and passes with each ordering weakened, and the models' pass is evidence through the weakened models that fail them; the count's clamp is unreachable, since every stored count is at most the ring's length; the 32-bit sequence admits a torn read only if a multiple of 2^31 writes fall between a reader's two loads; loom does not model load buffering (readers store nothing, so no load-store cycle exists) and keeps seven stores per atomic: the ring and front-growth models' readers may read five sequence values, a margin of two, the generation model's seven, no margin, so a further write there needs a model of its own; one reader, since a second only loads and a model of two exceeded loom's branch limit; the shipped `Native` orderings stay under Miri's 150-push stress test, which in the branch's review read torn samples with each of the four weakened. The nightly loom job runs it: `cargo test -p lactose-net-host --features loom --release --locked`, 21 s on the M1. The retry rate and the footprint: **in part** (branch `measure-m2`, round one and its fix round; not the record): `InterpolationReader::retried_reads` counts the reads a write overlapped, each once, by one `Relaxed` add on a retried read alone, held by a deterministic test of the reload path, the stress test's bound of one a read and a one-slot race of 2,000,000 pushes counted at least once; `InterpolationReader::bytes` is the slots' footprint. In the lane's fix-round `--all` run on the M1 (commit 7e3b62a, after fix rounds one and two), `remote-upload-5k` at load averages 4.48 5.07 6.19 before and 4.32 5.02 6.16 after: 528 bytes a slot, 2,640,000 for 5,000; paced, 132,340 of 7,200,000 reads retried (1.84e-2) under 326 sends at 30 Hz; unpaced, 1,093 of 7,200,000 (1.52e-4). In the foreground task role (D164; lane `measure-role`, item 5, commit 3a67a04, at load averages 15.43 23.21 15.77 before and 16.06 23.10 15.81 after): paced, 2,723 of 7,200,000 reads retried (3.78e-4) under 330 sends at 30 Hz, 98.4% of the frames on a performance core, none on an efficiency core and 1.6% moved between the classes; unpaced, 412 (5.72e-5), 98.3% on a performance core and 1.7% moved. The rate follows the batch call's length, so the frame's mode (row 19): in review r1 it read 8.58e-5 unpaced and 1.75e-2 to 1.86e-2 paced on the quieter machine | 2 |

| The reconciliation tolerance (D124): the milestone 1 spike's corrections per minute under the defaults for a dynamic capsule character on static geometry at 100 ms and 1% loss, the same with a kinematic character, fifty thrown claimed bodies, and a claim of a crate being pushed and of a stack being bumped; the largest absorbed difference per kind (`PredictionStats::absorbed`), and whether any single-contact maximum exceeds half a default, which doubles it (the probes' figures already do for translation and rotation, so those two are expected to go to 4e-3; the owner kept 2e-3 so the spike measures the shipped path first); and how long claimed bodies in mutual contact keep correcting after a throw. The probes' figures, at 20 Hz frames with every fact written at the rollback: a lone capsule one correction per misprediction (8 to 36 bit-exact), absorbing at most 2.4e-5 m; a capsule pushing a crate 1 to 2 (7 to 200), absorbing 7.5e-4 m and 5.3e-3 m/s; a rolled ball and a tipped crate 1 (1 to 200), absorbing 1.2e-3 m, 1.1e-3 per quaternion component and 3.4e-3 rad/s; a crate claimed mid-push 1 to 2, absorbing 1.9e-3 m; a three-crate stack 7 to 16 after a misprediction while it moves (8 to 200) and 22 to 35 in 167 frames when claimed mid-bump, once when corrected or claimed at rest. The probes' correction pushed bit-equal rows too and modelled no rollback; the spike measures the shipped path. The scalar decision re-sets none of the defaults (D146): the scalar stays 64-bit, the width the probes set them under and the spike measured them at. They are re-set from the spike's figures at milestone 4. Spike, corrections per minute under the defaults (server clock / clock script): dynamic capsule 0.1 / 1.0 (once at the join, tick 10, and 9 times around the stall's resync in cycle 1, from tick 488; never otherwise); kinematic character 0 / 0.9 (no join correction, since the kinematic body does not fall; the same 9 around the stall); claim in contact 338.8 / 348.1; claim on approach 61.4 (server clock; 4 to 6 per cycle). Fifty thrown bodies is a step, not a rate: on the server clock 0 in cycles 1 to 44, departure at tick 13,652, then 0 to 93 per cycle (mean 43.5) to the end, 326.9 per minute over ten minutes; under the clock script the stall's resync is the departure (tick 488), then 74 to 95 per throw cycle; on a perfect link the departure is tick 10,092. A server-clock run shorter than the departure reports about 0. The thrown substituted and repaired rates (17.8 and 3.0 per minute) come wholly from the scripted fault windows, since the link's jitter is order within a tick. All correction figures were taken with every claimed body in every update, unbudgeted. The claim scene's corrections are mostly claim timing and the proxy rule (server clock, 7,200 ticks, 641 corrections): 358 at the pushes before each claim (phases 80-100 and 150-160) and at the claim facts, 24 at the release, 1 at the join, 258 between claimed bodies in mutual contact. A claim after contact costs about 10 corrections per claim at 60 to 150 times the defaults, since the unclaimed crate is a kinematic proxy the predicted character cannot push. Claiming on approach removes the proxy blocking but not the contact-onset correction. The approach run (one crate claimed at phase 60, about 22 ticks before contact) corrects about 5 times a cycle (61.4 per minute, against 338.8 for the claim scene's four crates): at the claim (phase 60) and the release (260), neither a contact, and three times in the push (phases 82, 96 and about 114). The first contact tick corrects the crate at 57 times the angular default because of the crate's sleep state, which the rows do not carry (4.4 times with the crate unable to sleep; row "A claimed body's sleep state", below). Doubling every default moved the claim scene's rate 16% (320.5 to 269.5 per minute, 7,200 ticks), the thrown scene's 0% (326.9 to 333.9) and the approach run's 4% (61.4 to 58.8, 36,000 ticks; the onset corrections stay, and the absorbed maxima rise to fill the doubled tolerance), so the tolerance is not what drives any of the three. `PredictionStats::absorbed` counts a field within tolerance of a fact that mismatched in another field, so the approach run's maxima are not the residual of agreed facts: translation 65% and rotation 57% of the defaults overall, 14% and 14% in facts that agreed (linear 96% and angular at the tolerance in both). The spike gives no case for doubling; the decision waits on a sleep-state rule for claimed bodies and a re-measure. Result file `spike-net` carries the eight default replays' rates as `<scene>.<clock>.corrections_per_minute.mismatch` (thrown under the clock script: 772.6 per minute, the stall's departure at tick 488) and their absorbed maxima as `.absorbed.*`; the approach and doubled-tolerance figures above are not in it. Milestone 1's part is **Done**: the spike's figures under the defaults are recorded, and neither the scalar decision (D146) nor the spike re-sets a default. The doubling waits on the sleep-state rule for claimed bodies (the row "A claimed body's sleep state") and its re-measure. | 4 |
| Solver history departure in the prediction world (D124, spike-net). Once a client's solver history departs from the server's (after a resync, or later on a perfect link), every contact pile mispredicts for the rest of the session: the spike's fifty thrown bodies go from 0 to 30-95 mismatches per cycle and never recover, though every body despawns each cycle. A full frame does not carry state the server's history built (solver and activation state; a resting body's sleep state is one, row "A claimed body's sleep state", below): a client joined at tick 3001 or 6001 of a perfect-link run (server clock) mispredicts from the first throw after the join (ticks 3128, 6128), while one joined at tick 1 holds until tick 10,092. What departs a client joined at tick 1 is open; the candidates are the join's rebuild of the client's solver, the cycle-0 rollbacks at ticks 266 and 286, and the timing of bodies falling asleep in the pile. The lever is a solver-state baseline (the server's physics snapshot sent with a FULL, or a periodic re-baseline), not the tolerance. Proof: predict's milestone 4 review, after the trigger is identified | 4 |
| A claimed body's sleep state is not carried and not made equal at the claim (D124; found by the second review of `spike-net`). The rows carry no activation state, so a claim (proxy to dynamic) cannot make the two ends' sleep states equal, and contact with a resting claimed body mispredicts on its first tick by more than an order of magnitude: the spike's claim on approach corrects the crate at 57 times the angular default on its first contact tick in every cycle, and at 4.4 times with the crate unable to sleep (a probe, not the spike). Which world's body sleeps was not instrumented. The levers: the claim wakes the body identically on both ends; claimable bodies never sleep; or the activation state travels with the claim, or with the solver-state baseline of row "Solver history departure" (a baseline borne by a full frame does not cover a claim made in a delta). Decided at predict's milestone 4 review, with `lactose-net`'s claim policy | 4 |

| The keeper's timed wait on Windows (D144): built by lane `timer-windows` (`580910d` with the Windows spin at 50 µs, `f0b965c` at 500 µs, then the review rounds' fixes, `08ca8dd` and `c5ff381`). On Windows each frame-critical worker has a `TimedPark`, an auto-reset event and a high-resolution waitable timer, which the pool makes when it is built and closes in its `Drop`. `Sleep::park`'s step waits on both. Every wake of a worker that may be in the idle protocol sets its event through `Sleep::wake_worker`, whether or not the waker claimed it, and the keeper spins the last 500 µs there. Shown on the M1: the fourteen loom models pass; the keeper hand-off model fails with "lost hand-off" both when its wakers only unpark and when `wake_worker` sets no flag; Miri passes in both configurations; the Windows code passes `clippy -D warnings` for x86_64-pc-windows-msvc with and without loom; the macOS benchmark rows do not move beyond noise; on an emulation of the Windows wait, the shutdown hour-away test fails at `LIMIT` when `Shared::shut_down`'s wake loop only unparks. Shown on the Windows laptop (brief 5): 86 of 86 `lactose-task` tests pass at the lane's two commits. At `f0b965c`, the defect test `a_two_millisecond_deadline_is_not_held_to_the_timer_tick` (median lateness of fifty 2 ms deadlines under 7.8 ms, now in up to three batches) is the only test to catch the old wait put back, at a median of 13.5 ms; with `SetEvent` skipped, three hour-away tests in `tests/tasks.rs` (an earlier timer, pushed work, shutdown) fail at `LIMIT`, and `a_later_timer_does_not_hold_an_earlier_one_back` was killed at 300 s. Shown on the laptop at the merge `9c1e005` (brief 6): 86 of 86 `lactose-task` tests pass, and the defect test passes 20 of 20 stress runs. With `SetEvent` skipped, six tests fail at `LIMIT` and none is killed: `sleep::tests::a_claim_ends_a_timed_park_an_hour_from_its_deadline`, which passed vacuously before review round 1 because it claimed before its sleeper reached the wait; `a_later_timer_does_not_hold_an_earlier_one_back`; the three hour-away tests; and `shutdown_with_work_outstanding_finishes_running_jobs_and_drops_the_rest`. With the old wait put back, only the defect test fails, in all three batches, at medians of 13.5 to 13.6 ms. With the wake loops of `Pool::open_gate` and `Shared::shut_down` reduced to a plain unpark, the four tests that end in a shutdown fail at `LIMIT`; no test is shown to catch `Pool::open_gate`'s loop alone. The failure paths are not reached by any test. Measured on the laptop (brief 5, part 3), in two interleaved rounds with other work running: `whisk`'s 60 Hz timer P99 is 15832.5 and 15740.7 µs at `7b2e2e8` (4412 and 4422 of 5000 deadlines over 1 ms), 706.9 and 862.7 µs at `580910d`, and 533.0 and 502.9 µs at `f0b965c` (16 and 13 over 1 ms); `f0b965c`'s process CPU, from `QueryProcessCycleTime` over a busy core's measured cycle rate (a share of one busy core at that clock), is 0.0105 and 0.0036 s a second above `580910d`'s, so D144's rule (more than 0.015 s a second above, or a P99 not under 1 ms, sets the spin back to 50 µs and the figure to 2 ms) does not trigger. At 2 ms, 500 deadlines a second, the spin costs 0.124 and 0.109 s a second more than `580910d`. The wake row (P99 216.4, 208.4 and 199.5 µs in round 1; 135.1, 131.5 and 150.8 µs in round 2) and the makespan rows moved with the other load, not the tree, and every run, `7b2e2e8`'s included, fails their thresholds. These are `f0b965c`'s figures; the review rounds' fixes add a `SetEvent` on no path a measured phase takes. To measure on the desktop if it boots Windows: the same rows. Open until the desktop's file: whether the figure holds on a quiet machine. Measured by the laptop's brief 4, not by the lane: `SetWaitableTimer` clears a timer that fired unwaited (200 of 200 trials), and a wake through the event and the timer together costs about what `park` does. Not measured: the keeper at a frame worker's priority; a wait asked once for the time left less a fixed margin, which part B of the brief in `docs/plans/windows-timer-proposal.md`, section 5, measures; the spin's power cost; the `SetEvent` per frame worker that `Pool::open_gate` makes at the end of each `block_in_place`; a 1 ms period under a minimised window, which Microsoft's page for `timeBeginPeriod` says Windows 11 does not guarantee. The desktop boots Windows and another system (the owner's answer of 15 November 2026, D144), so the row has its closer: the desktop's Windows `whisk` file at the first commit of main that holds the lane, `9c1e005`, and at D144's follow-up if one is made, which D138's carried tick-start criterion reads against 1 ms, or 2 ms after the follow-up (D144). The list above is owed to no criterion (D149): it is read at milestone 2, where the first Windows client's frame pacing is decided (D144). The desktop's file is session A's and is written in milestone 2's desktop sitting, at those commits (D151). The unmeasured list was read by the architect of the client's frame, lane `frame-design` (D153, D162). Three items the frame uses are measured. The keeper at a frame worker's priority: result file `frame-pace`, as the lateness of each frame's first tick in a shown run on Windows, read beside the record's greatest width, which counts the jobs `lactose-presentation`'s `upload` forks in the frame's job with the record's, the keeper being the frame-critical worker that the frame's job and what it forks leave out (`Executor::run_job_width`, which D162 asks of `lactose-task`). The `SetEvent` for each frame worker that `Pool::open_gate` makes: `record-stall`'s Windows rows. A 1 ms period under a minimised window: `frame-pace`'s minimised run on Windows, with no threshold. The wait asked once for the time left less a fixed margin it does not use, and that item stays re-gated: it is a shape of the keeper's own wait. The frame's wait for the frame before it, which ends a margin before the next tick's deadline, is a second deadline kept by the keeper's wait as D144 measured it, on an executor of two frame-critical workers or more, and `frame-pace` gives the lateness of the ticks that follow such a wait; on one such worker a frame looks once at the frame before it and does not wait for it. The spin's power cost it uses more than milestone 1 did, in the periods that have that second deadline: `frame-pace` counts them, and the power's own measurement stays re-gated. Both are in a row of their own, with the reason | 2 |

A bare dynamic body gains an offset, tilted cuboid at tick 6 and is pushed in that tick (an impulse and a torque impulse); it reports, bit for bit, the states of a control created with that collider at every tick to 180, and lands on the floor (tick 52). Without the push the dropped rebuild passes, which is why the push is there | 1 |

| Tick start on macOS 27.2.1 (D154). The M1's re-run after the upgrade fails tick start's 35 µs (D138): `docs/results/whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-17.md` (`a539d60`, load averages 4.58 3.85 3.38 before and 2.97 3.97 3.67 after, the benchmark's own) gives at 60 Hz P50 8.5 µs, P99 36.2 µs, max 1,211.8 µs and one of 5,000 deadlines over 1 ms, against P50 2.9 µs and P99 24.4 µs at `0ec2d90` on macOS 14.7.4. Three full runs of the benchmark alone followed in the same window: `a539d60` twice, P50 8.7 and 8.3 µs, P99 36.6 and 38.3 µs (`whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-17-run2`, `-run3`), and `0ec2d90`, the old file's commit, P50 6.5 µs, P99 34.6 µs (`whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-17-at-0ec2d90`, load 12.40 before), none over 1 ms. It is not the tree: between the two commits no code of `lactose-task` or `lactose-core` changed, nor the lock file, the toolchain or the cargo configuration. It is not load as such: the four checked-in files of macOS 14.7.4, 9 October to 16 November at loads 1.51 to 5.71, give P50 2.6 to 3.7 µs and P99 24.4 to 29.9 µs, and the fastest of the four runs on 27.2.1 began at the highest load. The machine was not away: the one sample over 1 ms (09:57:36 UTC) falls on no sleep, wake or dark wake of `pmset -g log`. It is not the keeper's timed wait growing longer, by a control and not by a probe: the 2 ms period ends on waits of the same lengths with less time in hand before the deadline, and its median is 2.1 to 2.3 µs against 1.4 to 2.1, its P99 25.5 to 26.4 µs against 20.5 to 23.6. What moved is the cost of work that starts after an idle gap, and it grows with the gap: against the file at `0ec2d90` the process spends 8 to 9 µs more CPU per event in both rows at a 2 ms gap and 28 to 34 µs more at 60 Hz, and the wake from an idle pool is P99 33.0 to 33.8 µs against 30.4 to 32.8, still under 35. The cause inside the system is not found, and one thing is not separated from it: every run on 27.2.1 had a system daemon, `ecosystemd`, busy at background priority, about 40% of one core through that evening. Milestone 1 is not reopened (D154). The owner ruled on 17 November 2026, on the question of `docs/proposals/tick-start-macos-27.md`: remeasure, then restate. The remeasurement is three full runs in a quiet window once the daemon is quiet, or on 19 November 2026 as it is, each with the daemon's share of a core over it: the finding closes with no change if every one is under 30 µs at P99 at 60 Hz, and otherwise tick start on macOS is restated at 50 µs at P99, Linux keeping 35 µs and Windows 1 ms. **Done** by restatement (D156): the remeasurement of 17 November 2026 at `d290812` (`whisk-apple-m1-2026-11-17-at-d290812`, `-at-d290812-run2`, `-at-d290812-run3`; load averages 1.40 2.12 3.96 before the first and 2.17 3.08 3.50 after the last), the daemon idle with no CPU time over any run, gives at 60 Hz P50 2.7 to 3.6 µs and P99 24.3, 27.6 and 30.3 µs. The 60 Hz median is macOS 14.7.4's again and the P99 within 0.4 µs of its files, so the daemon's load explains most of the miss; the 2 ms period's P99, 26.5 to 33.8 µs, and the process's CPU per event at both periods stay above the file at `0ec2d90`, and the wake from an idle pool is P99 31.4 to 31.5 µs, under 35. One run of three is at 30 µs or over. Tick start is held to 50 µs at P99 on macOS; Linux keeps 35 µs and Windows 1 ms. It is read again at milestone 2's measurement commit, and under 30 µs there brings 35 µs back as a proposal | 2 |

| Which queue families of the laptop's devices, and of the desktop's from its first sitting, have the graphics bit and can present is `gpu-probe`'s to record. Where only the graphics queue's does, the present thread shares that queue under its lock (D153), and a blocking present there is a finding for the planner. It is the one place the present thread holds anything of the client's frame (D162). There no method of the render side that reaches the queue, `submit` and `open` with its empty submit, is called while a present call is in flight, so no frame-critical worker waits for the queue's lock behind a present: the render task awaits the call before its submit, and a frame that would ask `open` meanwhile awaits the call until a margin before the next tick's deadline, and is busy and defers its passes if the call has not returned by then. The frame's submit is held for what is left of that call, its wait for a swapchain image included, and a frame so held is submitted and never shown, as a frame is dropped at its submit on a queue of its own. The frames that begin while a render task is held run their ticks, wait for it until the same limit and defer their passes past it (`Rendered::Busy`), so the hold delays no tick within `log_retention − CATCH_UP_CAP` ticks, 1.9 s at the defaults and real time. MoltenVK on the M1 gives four families that present, so it never shares of itself (`reviewer-gpu`'s probe for D162's first draft); `reviewer-gpu` ran the frame's order there with both roles put on one queue, in the reviews of D162's second, third and fourth drafts, and D162 records the figures. Result file `frame-pace` gives, on each device that shares, the frames whose submit was held, the frames shed and the frames that were busy, the P50 and P99 of the hold, of each present call in three parts (the present side's own waits, the acquire, and the driver's submit and present under the queue's lock) and of the latch's two ages: at the display, where the swapchain reports display times, and at the return of the present call that showed it. A submit that began during a present's driver call, by the two calls' stamps, waited behind one on a worker, and where those submits' P99 is as long as the P99 of the driver call it is a defect; on two queues such a submit may have waited, and is a defect where those submits' P99 exceeds the P99 of the submits begun outside one by more than the latter's own spread from P50 to P99; a submit that began before a driver call and ran into it, or as long and begun outside one, is the submit's own cost. A hold much longer than the part of the present call under the queue's lock is the finding a thread for the submit would answer, which is a plan change (`crates/app/README.md`, "Not in this crate yet"). The latch's age at the return shows a call that blocked and nothing of an image that waits: with a frame period 0.1% shorter than the display's, `reviewer-gpu` measured the latch 54.4 ms old at the display at the median, against a floor of 22 to 31 ms, where its age at the return read 5.9 ms. The median age at the display is read against the P99 of the run's own swept floor: above it an image waits behind another, which is the shedding rule's steady state where display times are reported and is recorded; more than a refresh above it, or a greatest count of presents not yet displayed, as each present call begins, by their own display times, above `SHED_DEPTH`, is a finding | 2. **In part** (branch `measure-m2`, round three and its fix rounds; The results file, and the pass on the device's ring, remain owed to `extract-50k`: the ring is `lactose-renderer`'s (D161), which gives the pass `Renderer::sink`, a renderer made with no cooked shader, and `Renderer::open`, which returns the point to wait for and never waits, so the wait for a free region stays outside the measured pass. The ring's memory type and flags have no call in `rhi-api` or in the renderer: the file names them from the backend its harness links, for a buffer of `MemoryClass::Upload` with `BufferUsage::DEVICE_ADDRESS`, until rhi-api's ask (a) of D161's merge gives one. **Rescened by lane `snapshot-order`** (D164): the figures above are `present-impl`'s fill on a scene whose presented keys lay in chunk order. `cost.rs` now presents 5,000 rows, half of them moved, in four archetypes and sixteen regions, their keys interleaved across the chunks of both presented archetypes, which its tests assert at the timed scene, with 10,000 printed beside, and runs a third arm in which a presented row is despawned and another spawned at every tick, so that every pass sorts; the fill sorts the places once and keeps them (D157's addendum at `snapshot-order`). At load averages 9.14 10.22 8.04 before and 9.37 10.24 8.07 after, one run, 60 passes an arm, the medians: at 5,000 presented rows, one worker, 0.93 ms without the snapshot, 1.01 ms with it (its part 0.08 ms) and 1.13 ms with one whose order breaks at every tick (0.20 ms); four workers 0.48, 0.62 (0.13 ms) and 0.73 ms (0.24 ms). At 10,000: one worker 0.92, 1.10 (0.17 ms) and 1.37 ms (0.45 ms); four workers 0.48, 0.74 (0.26 ms) and 0.92 ms (0.44 ms). A second run at load averages 19.51 27.62 28.11 before and 19.31 27.44 28.04 after: at 5,000, one worker 0.96, 1.03 (0.06 ms) and 1.33 ms (0.36 ms), four workers 0.52, 0.68 (0.16 ms) and 0.77 ms (0.25 ms); at 10,000, one worker 1.07, 1.20 (0.14 ms) and 1.35 ms (0.29 ms), four workers 0.50, 0.74 (0.24 ms) and 1.05 ms (0.55 ms). At the fix round's head, whose fill pushes each row through a callback, one run at load averages 18.84 18.34 14.99 before and after: at 5,000, four workers 0.48, 0.51 (0.03 ms) and 0.60 ms (0.12 ms), one worker unreadable, the arm without the snapshot slower than the arm with it; at 10,000, one worker 0.91, 1.07 (0.16 ms) and 1.35 ms (0.44 ms), four workers 0.47, 0.69 (0.22 ms) and 0.79 ms (0.32 ms). The snapshot's half is serial, so with four workers its part is a serial tail and reads larger than with one; the part is a difference of two medians and moves with the load. The review's baseline on the same scene, `present-impl`'s fill swapped back in (release, load averages 9.98 before and 9.58 after): its part 0.47 ms on one worker and 0.41 ms on four at 5,000 presented rows, 1.01 ms and 0.97 ms at 10,000. | The snapshot channel's triple buffer (D157) is proven by a property test over sequences of write, publish and acquire, with `Arc::get_mut` asserting that the buffer the writer writes is shared with nobody, under a reader that acquires every publication, every second, never, once per two publications while the second pass writes the first's taken-back buffer, and once per pass inside its span. After every step it checks: a held snapshot is never written and no pass waits; a reader that acquires every publication gets the two newest published; after the first publication no pair holds one snapshot twice except the reader's newer while a pass writes a snapshot it took back, and never two such pairs in a row, and a reader that acquires once per pass inside its span gets one every second acquire and never a pair backward in time; the first acquire whose newer snapshot was published since a cut has a cut as its newer one, also when a snapshot was taken back, replaced or published after it, and when the cut itself was taken back; the mark is passed by the third acquire after it that follows a publication, and in the slow cadence after one pair; a snapshot of a tick earlier than the snapshot published last is a cut in every kind, so no pair's older snapshot is of a later tick than its newer one unless that one is a cut; a prediction world's full pass at the tick published last takes that snapshot's place, keeps its cut and pairs from what it followed; a snapshot a second pass updates before `publish` keeps a cut and a full mark; with the reader dropped, `publish` keeps no event. It is not loom and not Miri: the exchange is safe Rust under one lock. Its text in the crate is `crates/present-api/README.md`'s proof row "The triple buffer". Owed by lane `present-impl` | 2. **Done:** `crates/present-api/tests/exchange.rs`, ten tests. Every write is a real pass whose presented row holds the write's number, checked against a model of the README's rules after every acquire, under the five cadences and 60 random sequences per kind (asserted to reach window pairs, take-backs, marks and, in a prediction world, replacements), with the cut taken back and replaced. Across threads: the render side on its own thread; each emission in one frame, with every emission published with or before its newer snapshot's first publication; a driven window pair. The reader-dropped case is `frame::with_the_reader_gone_publish_keeps_no_event`. A mark cleared only by a snapshot published after it fails seven of the tests. Publishing the snapshot and its events, or taking the pair and the events, in two exchanges fails `exchange::events_reach_one_frame_with_their_snapshot_across_threads`: with a yield between the two exchanges, in every run; with none, in most runs (of 20 runs each, taking them apart failed 18 and 20, publishing them apart 17 and 12, in fix round 2 and in review round 2) |

| What a reader that stops acquiring while its world keeps publishing receives when it acquires again (a hidden or minimised window, a hitch, a debugger). Today it receives every event of the pause, folded, in one frame, and the channel keeps one batch per publication with an event meanwhile; nothing done under the lock grows with that backlog (`crates/present-api/README.md`, "Events and publication"). Whether a long pause drops, caps or delivers those events, and whether a hidden window acquires at all, is settled by `frame-design` (D162): the client's frame acquires once in every frame that published and in no other, so its reader never stops while its world publishes and no backlog forms. A hidden window publishes, acquires and delivers in every frame, with the sink detached. A frame that waits, for a region or while the frame before it is still on the render side (`Rendered::Busy`), publishes nothing, and its world's events stay in the world's queue, those of `log_retention` ticks at most, for the publication that follows; a process stopped in a debugger publishes nothing either. No event is dropped or capped. Owed by `frame-impl`: a test with a render side that logs its calls, which finds one acquire after each `publish` and none elsewhere through each of the frame's three states and through a busy frame, and every event of a waiting span delivered once **Advanced** (branch `frame-impl`, round one): `crates/app/tests/frame/states.rs`, `the_frame_carries_the_presentation_events_of_its_ticks`, finds each frame that runs its passes handed the events of every tick since the last frame that did, one a tick for each row, and a frame that waited for a region handed none, the frame after it both ticks' once; `tests/frame/pacing.rs` finds a busy frame calling no method of the render side and publishing nothing; and criterion 3(a) (`tests/shown.rs`, section 5) finds the next `present` after a pass reading a snapshot of that pass's tick or later, through each state and a busy frame. The publication and the acquire are no calls of a side, so the logged `present` that reads the pair stands for the acquire; no test counts the events of a span of busy frames | 2 |

| The main-thread lane's latency (D159): a call queued while the operating system holds the main thread in a modal loop, a live resize or a title-bar drag on Windows, a menu or a live resize on macOS, runs when the loop next runs the pump. How long that is, and whether a wake-up is delivered inside the modal loop, is not measured. Nothing per-frame goes through the lane, so the cost is a late start, a late `request_size` or a late `exit`. **The tool is built; the owner's runs are owed** (branch `platform-impl`). `cargo run -p lactose-platform --example main_lane_gap -- [SECONDS]` opens a window and queues a main-lane call every 5 ms from another thread; each call sends when it was queued and when it ran on a channel, so the main thread waits on nothing of the tool's. It prints, for each second over the calls that ran in it and then for the whole run, the longest wait of a call from its queueing to its run, the longest gap between two runs (counted from the last run of the second before), the longest gap between two queueings, which is the queueing thread's own cadence on a loaded machine, and the age of the oldest call still waiting, so a second in which the main thread is held prints "0 ran" and that age. The owner, at the machine, live-resizes the window by a corner on the M1 and drags it by its title bar on the laptop, holding each still with the button down for a few seconds, and records the lines of the seconds that cover it and the last line (input is not synthesised, since a synthesised drag may not enter the modal loop a hand does). The figure is the longest wait and the longest gap between runs in the seconds that cover the hold, read against the longest gap between queueings, which is the tool's own cadence. A main-lane call that holds the main thread for 2.2 s from 3.5 s, in place of a hand, shows as the oldest waiting call at 494 ms and 1.5 s in the fourth and fifth seconds, and as a 2.2 s wait and gap in the sixth. Untouched on the M1 for 4 s, with other builds running (load averages 10.8, 7.7, 6.4), 644 calls ran of 644 queued; in the first second, as the window came up, a call waited 65 ms and the longest gap between runs was 10.4 ms; from the second on, no call waited over 86 µs and the longest gap between runs, 6.3 ms, was the gap between queueings | 2 |

| The client's frame is paced by the timer root (D162): one frame at each tick deadline, drawn at a fraction near zero, so the picture is the state of the tick before the newest and a display faster than 60 Hz repeats frames. A frame waits for the frame before it until `lactose_app::JOIN_MARGIN`, 2 ms, before the next tick's deadline, and draws as soon as that frame's render task resolves; a frame that finds it still on the render side then has run its ticks and draws nothing (`Rendered::Busy`), so a record that outlasts the tick period costs pictures, and no tick where a frame-critical worker is left to start it. The frame's job and what it forks occupy all the frame-critical workers but one, and one where there is one (`Executor::run_job_width`, asked of `lactose-task` with `run_job`): the worker left out fires the timers, is the keeper and runs the root. That costs a record that would fill W workers W/(W − 1) of its time, the job's own worker running its scope's children itself under the place it holds. In `reviewer-concurrency`'s runs for D162's fourth draft, on four workers with no width and a stand-in record of 17.5 ms whose fan-out filled them, the ticks started 13.3 to 13.7 ms late at P99; cut for three workers that record is 22.2 ms. In its runs for the fifth draft, on `lactose-task` as it is with the record cut by hand so that one worker stayed out, the record took 21.73 ms on four workers and the frame drew 43.6 pictures a second, its ticks 0.001 to 0.004 ms late at the median. Two ignored tests of `lactose-task`'s `tests/jobs.rs` measure two different things. `fan_out_time_under_the_width` times the fan-out's throughput under the width: the scope's own time, 100 samples a shape. In two runs at load averages of 7.0 to 8.1, its P50 in ms in `run_job` against a graph job, and their ratio, was 20.01 against 10.00 to 10.02 (2.00) for four children of 10 ms, which take two rounds on three workers; 10.00 to 10.01 against 10.00 (1.00) for three children of 10 ms; 13.42 to 13.46 against 10.52 to 10.66 (1.26 to 1.28) for a range of 2,000 units of 20 µs; and 14.01 to 14.02 against 10.01 to 10.06 (1.39 to 1.40) for forty children of 1 ms, the 14 rounds against 10 that three workers need. A fan-out cut for three workers pays 4/3 at most; one that is not pays its rounds. `timed_wake_inside_a_fan_out` times a timer and not the fan-out: the lateness of a frame-critical task's `sleep_until` 2 ms ahead, set once the fan-out has begun, 200 samples a shape. In four runs at load averages of 3.3 to 8.3, its lateness at P50, P99 and the greatest, in ms, was, for four children of 10 ms, 0.001 to 0.003, 0.013 to 7.77 and 0.029 to 7.98 in `run_job` against 0.003 to 0.004, 8.00 to 8.01 and 8.01 in a graph job; for the range, 0.002 to 0.008, 0.13 to 3.18 and 0.16 to 7.74 against 1.84 to 3.02, 8.05 to 9.53 and 8.08 to 11.26; and for forty children of 1 ms, 0.002 to 0.003, 0.013 to 1.29 and 0.22 to 4.47 against 0.001 to 0.005, 0.008 to 0.41 and 0.012 to 0.83, a fan-out whose children end often enough that the graph job's workers fire the timer between them. The greatest figures of `run_job`, up to 8 ms, are a timed park waking late, not a missed timer. `reviewer-concurrency` traced 43 samples late by over 1 ms, in each of which the free worker held the timer with the right deadline in a timed wait: either that wait returned late (asked 1.07 to 1.17 ms, it took 1.4 to 12.3 ms), or the keeper was not scheduled until a child ended, and the worker leaving the child fired the timer within 2 µs. A control with no executor, the same timed park on a frame-critical thread beside three spinning frame-critical threads, gave over 800 samples a P99 of 7.20 ms, a greatest of 8.06 ms and 43 over 1 ms: three spinners at user-interactive QoS on four performance cores, under the other agents' load, leave the keeper waiting up to a scheduler quantum for a core. What only a device or another desktop can show is the same on Windows, whose timed waits are coarser, and with the renderer's record in place of the stand-in. The stand-in is not the renderer's record, whose recording jobs no run has timed. On an executor of one frame-critical worker a frame looks once at the frame before it and does not wait, and a tick whose deadline falls inside the record's job waits for the job. In `reviewer-gpu`'s probe for D162's third draft, under MoltenVK on the M1 with a queue of its own, a record of 17 ms showed 56.8 pictures a second and one of 20 ms 48.4, where a look that waited for nothing showed 30.0 at each; its ticks started within half a millisecond of their deadlines at P99 by the probe's own timed wake, which says nothing of a record that fills the workers. Result file `frame-pace` (`measure-m2`), from a shown run of `lactose-app-client` of ten seconds or more on the M1 and on Windows, and on Windows from the same run minimised, gives for the paced frames the P50 and P99 lateness of the first tick's start against the deadline the root slept to, how many frames drew, waited for a region, were busy, hid and were torn, how many presents were queued and shown, how many submits were held and how many frames were shed, the frames that reported `behind`, and the P50 and P99 of the record, of the record's longest job (the longest of the closures the render side records in or forks, a figure of the record's cost), of `RenderSide::submit`'s time, of a shared queue's hold, of each present call in three parts (the present side's own waits, the acquire, and the driver's submit and present under the queue's lock) and of the latch's age, twice, beside the greatest width any record reached (the most frame-critical workers inside the frame's job at one moment, the jobs `lactose-presentation`'s `upload` forks in `present` counted with the record's), the device's queue layout, the count of frame-critical workers and the width. The first age is at the display, by the swapchain's display-time report, read against the run's own floor, which is the same binary in the same sitting with its frames a thousandth slower than half the display's rate, so that no image waits behind another and the frames' phase against the refresh is swept; both of those runs last a minute or more. The display's rate is the display mode's exact figure, not one rounded to whole hertz and not a swapchain's fallback (MoltenVK 1.4.2 reports its refresh from an integer, `NSScreen.maximumFramesPerSecond`, where the mode gives none), and the file records it with its source and how many times the floor run swept: a display at 59.94 Hz taken as 60 leaves the frames at exactly half its rate, on one phase, so a run that swept fewer than twice is no floor, is recorded as that, and is made again with its frames set from a refresh fitted to the display times. The second is at the return of the present call that showed it. Where the swapchain reports no display time the file says so and has the second alone. It records what moves those figures: the load averages before and after the run, the swapchain's image count, whether a validation layer was on, and the display's state (its refresh rate, whether the window was occluded, whether another client was drawing). Each row is run once on a quiet machine. The lateness is read against D144's 1 ms and D156's 50 µs, which are criteria of a tick's start with idle workers: a P99 over one in a shown run is a finding for the planner and no failed criterion, the count of frames over it given beside it. Four readings are fixed. On every queue layout the submits that began during a present's driver call, by the two calls' stamps, are counted, a submit that began before one and ran into it being its own cost. On a shared queue such a submit waited behind the driver call on a worker, and where those submits' P99 is as long as the P99 of the driver call it is a defect to report; on two queues it may have waited behind one, where a driver serialises its queues, and it is a defect to report where those submits' P99 exceeds the P99 of the submits begun outside one by more than the latter's own spread from P50 to P99, the excess given as a figure. A submit as long that began outside one is the submit's own cost and is recorded. On a shared queue a hold much longer than the part of the present call under the queue's lock is what a thread for the submit would remove. The latch's median age at the display is read against the swept floor's P99: above it an image waits behind another, which is the shedding rule's steady state and is recorded, and more than a refresh above it, or a greatest count of presents not yet displayed, as each present call begins, by their own display times, above `SHED_DEPTH`, is a finding; its age at the return shows a call that blocks and nothing of a backlog. And `busy` is read beside the record's P99, and the lateness beside the record's greatest width and by what the frame before did: a lateness beside a greatest width equal to the count of frame-critical workers is a finding for `lactose-task` or for the binary's worker count, which `JOIN_MARGIN` does not mend and the record's longest job cannot show, a worker that drains a range or runs its scope's children firing no timer between closures; and with no such record a lateness above zero after a frame that waited is what changes `JOIN_MARGIN`. Where the present role has a queue of its own the decision is at the frame's submit, when the present call before it has not returned. Where it shares the graphics queue the frame's submit is held for that call, and the frame is dropped after the submit. Where a present outlasts a frame, every second frame or more is never shown. `frame-pace`'s counts of frames drawn, held, shed and busy and of presents queued and shown say whether it happens under MoltenVK on the M1 and on the laptop's devices, and only beside what the file records with them. | The client's frame takes back a finished target that no present read by importing it from `Undefined`, which must end a release toward the present role that no acquire met (D160's item 7 (c)), and gives every submit that uses a target a wait for the present's last read of it (item 7 (a)) (D162). Such a re-take, and one after a torn frame, follows a write on the frame's own queue and not a present's read, so a Vulkan backend's barrier from undefined carries every stage and every write access in its source scope (`ALL_COMMANDS`, `MEMORY_WRITE`). `crates/rhi-api/README.md`, "States", says both: a transition from `Undefined` "ends a release no acquire met and takes the resource with no transfer", and "Vulkan gives such a transition a source scope of every stage and every write access". Its conformance suite's output case `retake_after_a_store_on_one_queue_is_clean` holds the second on a device, under synchronisation validation. Null ends such a release (`crates/render-graph/README.md`, "What each consumer needs"); it tracks states and not availability, so it cannot show a barrier that leaves the write out. On a shared queue every held frame is dropped, and on either layout every shed frame is, so the case is an everyday one and not a slow present's alone. In `reviewer-gpu`'s runs for D162's second draft, with both roles on one queue under the Khronos layer's synchronisation and thread-safety validation, a source scope of every stage and no access was reported as `SYNC-HAZARD-WRITE-AFTER-WRITE`, 10 errors over 93 dropped frames in one run and 1 after 1 in another; with `MEMORY_WRITE` nothing was reported in three runs, every readback exact. Owed by `frame-impl`: a run on `lactose-rhi-null` on each queue layout through each of the frame's three states and a busy frame, with a present side that skips every second call and, on the shared layout, held frames dropped, refused nothing; the same under the Khronos layer's synchronisation validation on a device whose graphics and present roles have two queues, which MoltenVK on the M1 is, with four families that present; and under the same validation on one queue for both roles, which lavapipe has, with held frames, a present call that makes no submit and a torn frame in the run, no message. The layer reports the write after a write; it does not report a re-take after a present's read whose barrier names too narrow a source stage, which ran unreported in `reviewer-gpu`'s probe for D162's first draft, so a clean one-queue run is evidence of the first alone **Advanced** (branch `frame-impl`, round two). A copy whose re-take assumes the call before read the target fails all three. A waited and a busy frame, and one torn by a refused submit, are not reached through the client's wiring on null, whose steps are joined (`crates/app/tests/frame/` drives each with no device); one torn by a target's refused make is, since fix round 3. On the M1 the window's devices give both layouts without forcing: MoltenVK 1.4.2 `QueueLayout { compute: Own, present: Own }`, KosmicKrisp `{ compute: Shared, present: Shared }`. A probe build of a scratch copy, whose present calls each sleep 40 ms, every 13th returning before its acquire with no submit, and whose every 97th draw is torn after its prepare, ran paced for 20 s under the layer's core, synchronisation and thread-safety validation, each canary heard in a process of its own. KosmicKrisp, one queue: 1,201 frames, 448 queued, 431 held, 312 busy, 34 calls with no submit, 9 torn, no message (load 23.59 17.45 16.90 before, 19.67 17.04 16.77 after). MoltenVK, a present queue of its own, with Metal API and shader validation too: 1,201 frames, 390 queued, 798 dropped, 30 calls with no submit, 12 torn, no message (load 19.67 17.04 16.77 before, 16.76 16.62 16.63 after). Lavapipe and the desktop are owed (a row below 241), and what the runs do not show is the row after it | 2 |
| A frame that waits, for a region or while the frame before it is still on the render side, defers every pass of its world, the replication gather among them, for `log_retention − CATCH_UP_CAP` ticks at most (D162). After that a frame with no region is hidden until one is free, and a frame behind a render task awaits it. A local world's gathered frame goes nowhere, so nothing is late. A shown world with clients needs its gather every frame whatever the device does, and `lactose-sim` runs the passes as one schedule: the change that shows a server with clients splits the gather from extract first | 4 |
| `lactose-app-client` is both a shipped binary and a harness package (D162: criterion 3(c)'s recorded test is in the module `recorded` of its `integration` target, since that package alone may name the real render side in normal code). `cargo xtask features` then compares its boundary tree in full with its dev-dependencies included, against `lactose-app-headless`'s. Owed by `frame-impl`: the comparison passes with `lactose-rhi-null` and the test's other dev-dependencies in the tree. If a dev-dependency moves the boundary tree, that is a finding for the planner, and D162's placement of the test is what changes **Answered** (branch `frame-impl`, fix rounds 1 and 2). At round two `cargo xtask features` reported six findings, one cause: `bitflags` 2.13.2, in the boundary tree through `lactose-physics-rapier` (`parry3d-f64` builds it without `std`), was built with `std` for `lactose-app-client` and without it for the other roots, on aarch64-apple-darwin and x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu. No dev-dependency moved it, so D162's placement of the test did not: with normal edges alone (`cargo tree -e normal,features -i bitflags`) the window's dependencies ask `std`, `objc2-app-kit` under winit through `lactose-platform` and `objc2-quartz-core` under `raw-window-metal` through `lactose-rhi-vulkan` on macOS, and `rustix` on Linux, so any feature root that links a window moves the tree. `lactose-physics-rapier` names `bitflags` through the workspace, pinned `=2.13.2` with `default-features = false, features = ["std"]`, so every root builds it alike, and `crate-tiers.toml` lists it among the crate's direct dependencies with that reason. `std` keeps a parse error's text and implements `std::error::Error` for it, and changes no flag value, bit operation or iteration order. On the branch `cargo xtask features` and `cargo xtask boundary` both pass, and with them `lactose-xtask`'s `workspace::the_feature_check_passes_with_no_roots_and_reads_real_trees`; `boundary::tests::the_physics_backend_may_not_name_the_solvers_math`, which reads the real tier file, expects `bitflags` on the crate's list with the rest; `cargo xtask vendor` and `cargo deny check` accept the pin. A pin moves with `parry3d-f64`'s `bitflags` | 2 |

A minimised window there reports an empty size and its surface a zero current extent: `lactose-rhi-vulkan` then makes no driver swapchain, its `acquire` answers `Stale` and a `resize` once the extent is not zero makes one (its README, "A zero extent"), a decision its unit test holds with no driver; the laptop's minimise case is the first run to meet it | 2 |
| A present that fails other than by `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DATE_KHR` or a loss (`lactose-rhi-vulkan`, "The swapchain", whose table states each error's fence by the spec and under MoltenVK 1.4.2): the backend takes it as `Presented::Stale`, the swapchain stale until a `resize`. Out of host or device memory enqueues nothing, by the spec, and its fence never signals: the fence is taken back, and the image's present semaphore, left signalled, is waited for by an empty batch. An error the spec does not list is waited for as a shown present's, and where its fence has not signalled a bound after the present, with the present queue idle, that is taken to show the present enqueued nothing: the fence is destroyed, and the image's present semaphore, with no wait pending, with its chain. MoltenVK 1.4.2's `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_POOL_MEMORY` is such an error: by its source it signals the fence where the drawable timed out (`MVKImage.mm` 1625; `MVKQueue::submit`, "Submit regardless of config result"), and that fence is reaped as any other, and never where the present had no command buffer (`MVKQueue.mm` 739–750), the one known source of a fence given up. **Shown by injection** (branch `swapchain`): the unit test `a_present_refused_with_nothing_enqueued_is_stale_until_a_resize`, a device test of `cargo xtask gpu` on a headless surface, makes the present answer each of the three errors with nothing enqueued; each leaves the swapchain stale until a `resize`, after which a present is shown, and the layer is silent for all three, to the device's destroy. **Open:** no driver here could be made to fail a present of itself; the enqueued errors' fences and an unlisted error's late signal are by inspection | 3 |
| The ring's write cost and `Renderer::prepare`'s cost, the steering D161 and the crate README's proof owe, as `renderer-impl`'s `tests/cost.rs` measures them on null: ignored tests, run in release with `--test-threads 1`, printing and asserting only counts. On the M1 at loads of 13.41 7.05 11.21 before and 20.48 9.75 12.01 after, 21 frames each: the ring's write of 50,000 rows in batches of 256, a median of 0.347 ms (maximum 0.802 ms); `prepare`, a median of 4.260 ms (maximum 5.458 ms) at 50,000 incremental rows among 100,000 held, 8.660 ms (maximum 11.744 ms) at a full pass of 100,000 held keys, and 21.412 ms (maximum 28.313 ms) at a load that replaces 100,000 held keys with 100,000 new ones. Null's buffers are host memory, so the write figure is the writers' own cost; a device's upload memory and a quiet machine are owed, at the device round and on the desktop | 2 |

| A render task suspended at its hold on a shared queue leaves the gate of the physics step's `block_in_place` to the ticks at `RECORD_RESERVATION` (D162; `crates/app/README.md`, "The targets and the present thread"). **Held**, for `lactose-app` (branch `frame-impl`, fix round 1) and for `lactose-task`'s own (branch `gate-task`). `crates/app/tests/frame/protocol.rs`, `a_held_render_task_leaves_the_workers_to_the_ticks_and_a_job`, runs on two frame workers the ticks of three busy frames beside a render task suspended at its hold, each tick's physics step calling `block_in_place` at the reservation `WorldConfig::shown` sets (`crates/physics-api/src/sync.rs`), and a job of `Executor::run_job` whose units wait for those ticks; a hold that blocks its worker until the call returns fails it at its bound (mutant `hold-blocks-worker`, which also fails `an_executor_of_one_frame_worker_looks_in_every_frame` and `stop_with_a_frame_in_flight_after_a_shutdown_is_cancelled`). `lactose-task`'s own: a task woken while a block holds the gate, as a render task is when its present call returns during a physics step, is polled only by a worker that holds a run permit within the limit, and its wake is not lost (`crates/task/README.md`, "The gate and the reservation" and the paragraph on tasks at the gate after "How the width is kept"). `crates/task/src/executor/gate_tests.rs` holds it by order, never by a time, through two hooks only the crate's tests compile, one just before a worker parks and one before a permitted worker looks for work: the task woken from a thread that is no worker, by a job beside the block, by a job begun before the block and by its timer; a wake, and the gate's opening, landed between a worker's look round and its park; and a task taken by a worker that passed its check of the gate as the gate closed. With no permit reserved the task is polled once the gate opens, the blocking worker still in its job; with one, at the closed gate by the worker the reservation leaves awake. The last case was a defect, fixed on `gate-task`: a worker over the limit polled a task it took, where it put a job back. It now puts the task back at the back of its pool's queue unpolled and gives its permit up at its next check of the gate; a task its timer woke and the firing worker kept on its own deque goes back to the queue the same way (`pool::tests::a_task_taken_at_a_closed_gate_is_put_back_unpolled`, `a_task_kept_from_its_timer_at_a_closed_gate_goes_back_to_the_queue`). The put-back wakes nobody: a sleeper held just before its park is still announced once the worker that put the task back is about to park (`a_task_put_back_over_the_limit_wakes_no_sleeper`). A task taken over the limit after shutdown began is swept by its own put-back, since shutdown's first sweep may have passed and a shutdown that does not join sweeps only once (`pool::tests::a_task_taken_after_shutdown_began_is_swept_by_its_put_back`). The loom model `a_task_put_back_at_a_closed_gate_is_polled_within_the_limit` runs the gate's shipped functions around a copy of the put-back, exhaustively, and fails beside `weakened_a_put_back_that_keeps_the_permit` and, as a deadlock, beside `weakened_a_put_back_between_the_look_and_the_park`; `a_task_put_back_beside_a_retaking_opener_is_polled_within_the_limit` adds a third worker that retakes a permit regardless of the limit, as an opener does, which makes second put-backs, at four preemptions. Seven mutants each fail at least one of these tests: a worker without a permit polling a queued task, a gate's opening that wakes no sleeper, the announcement of a sleep moved after its look round, the poll over the limit the fix removes, the check of the gate after a put-back dropped, the put-back's shutdown sweep dropped, and a put-back that wakes a sleeper. With `frame-impl` merged in a trial (`gate-task` 73b986e with `frame-impl` beafcd2, and at this merge with `frame-impl` 3b60728 on main), `lactose-app` and `lactose-app-client` passed, this row's test among them. On the M1 the thirteen tests of this branch, with `a_job_taken_at_a_closed_gate_is_put_back_unstarted` beside them, each passed 1,000 nextest stress runs, at one-minute load averages 10.4 to 33.4, a release build of the workspace beside the first 175 of the 240 seconds. After fix round 1 those fourteen, the two tests it adds and `a_job_taken_after_shutdown_began_never_starts`, seventeen in all, each passed 300 more, at one-minute load averages 2.9 to 4.6, and `a_task_put_back_over_the_limit_wakes_no_sleeper` passed 1,000 alone at 11.6 to 12.3. | 2 |
| Criterion 1 on the Windows laptop (decision 5.1, D162): `lactose-app-client --fixed 600` on each of the laptop's GPUs, chosen by `VK_LOADER_DRIVERS_SELECT`, under the Khronos layer's core, synchronisation and thread-safety validation, each validator's canary run in a process of its own beside it. **Owed, the owner's**: the commands from a clean checkout are the coordinator's, from lane `frame-impl`, and `cargo xtask gpu` does not run there, since the pinned layer has no Windows build. | The ring's memory type in a device file's header (`measure-m2`, D162 addendum): the header names the type `lactose-rhi-vulkan`'s rule picks from the device's memory types as `vulkaninfo` lists them, not the type the ring's buffer was given, whose memory-type bits no surface reports. On the M1 they are one: under MoltenVK type 1 (0x000f) is the only host-visible type of three, and KosmicKrisp has one type, so a mapped ring has no other. Where several types are host-visible and coherent (a discrete GPU's), the buffer's bits decide. **Open** until a surface of `lactose-rhi-vulkan` reports the ring's type, or until each of the desktop's and the laptop's devices lists one such type | 2 |
| GPU timestamps on the M1's drivers (`lactose-rhi-vulkan`'s `Timestamps`; D162 addendum at `measure-m2`, round two): MoltenVK 1.4.2 and KosmicKrisp may latch a timestamp at a boundary of a Metal pass near its write, not where the queue's order puts it. MoltenVK writes it as a Metal pass of its own: with the first in an encoder of its own and the second in a render pass's encoder after the pass, in one submit or with the first in a submit of its own, it latched the second at a quarter point of a 21 ms pass in some rounds (0.24 or 0.75 of the host's time). KosmicKrisp read that pass in every layout tried, and around a dispatch it read under 0.12 ms with both writes in the dispatch's encoder. Each write in an encoder submitted alone, the work's submit between them and each submit waiting on the one before, read the work under both drivers: `timestamps_around_a_lagging_dispatch_read_its_lag` and `timestamps_in_submits_around_a_lagging_render_pass_read_its_lag`, the second over 16 rounds, each read between 0.9 of the least host time less 2 ms and its own host time, a band a quarter-point latch fails; round three's layout fails it under MoltenVK in every run. The pair reads the work with the queue's handoffs between the three submits, about 0.4 ms under MoltenVK and 0.7 ms under KosmicKrisp around an empty pass on the M1, which `gpu-cost`'s `clear` set reads by itself. MoltenVK made the results available about 1.6 ms after a wait saw the submit's signal, so `gpu-cost` and the device tests poll `read`, bounded at 10 s. `gpu-cost` uses that layout. **Open** for every other driver: both device tests run under `cargo xtask gpu` wherever the gate runs, the laptop's and the desktop's drivers not yet among them, and `rhi-metal` reads its own | 2 |
| Where macOS starts a frame-critical worker's paced job, and at what clock (D164's placement row). `remote-upload-5k`'s job is 0.3 ms of work every 6.9 ms. In the runs of lane `measure-m2` and its first review on the M1 under macOS 27.2.1 it took 0.32 to 0.34 ms on a performance core at full clock, 0.55 to 0.65 ms on one at reduced clock and 1.4 to 1.9 ms on an efficiency core at reduced clock; in a slow run 804 to 1,106 of 1,440 jobs began on efficiency cores, seven runs back to back gave medians from 0.32 to 1.84 ms, and the load average did not say which. Every one of those runs was started from a terminal, so in the `TASK_UNSPECIFIED` role, where user-interactive QoS is clamped to priority 31 (row 78), and its frame held nothing else. A stand-in of the proposal's review, plain threads at user-interactive QoS and not the executor, ran the four cases, with the machine in two states, its performance cores' chain at 16.6 to 22.5 µs or at 9.7 µs. In the foreground role a job after 3 ms of work on three other threads began on a performance core in every frame of 6 runs of 6, at the slower clock in the slower state; alone it began on efficiency cores in 1,060 and 1,161 of 1,440 frames in the slower state and in at most 1 in the faster. In the unspecified role 871 to 1,152 of 1,440 began there in the slower state and 271 to 315 in the faster, busy or alone. Not measured: any of this through the executor and the timer root; the role a client started as an application bundle is given (`docs/results/spike-wake-apple-m1-2026-10-06.md`, "Not tested"). Owed: a phase of `lactose-task`'s benchmark. A 144 Hz timer root runs a job of fixed safe work, 0.3 ms at full clock, through `run_job`, in the four cases, each role alone and after a stand-in that keeps the job's other workers busy for 3 ms, 1,440 frames a case, in five interleaved rounds or more, a case in its own process where the role cannot be given back. For each case and round the file gives the share of jobs that began on each class of core, each class's calibration and the job's median. The rule is read on the busy case, the class of core and the clock apart, and an outcome needs every round. Under a tenth of the jobs on efficiency cores in the foreground role, in every round: no placement means is built; and if the unspecified role puts over a tenth there in every round, the role is what holds the cores, and the planner proposes with milestone 3's plan how a client started outside an application bundle takes it. Over a tenth in the foreground role, in every round: the planner proposes a placement means before milestone 3 fixes a frame budget on the M1, a parallel workgroup first (`os_workgroup_parallel_create`; the SDK's `os/workgroup_interval.h` gives a frame no work interval). Rounds that disagree decide nothing: they are made once more on another day, and the planner reads both sets. The performance cores' calibration in the busy case is recorded as the clock a frame gets, which no placement means sets. The cases alone are recorded and decide nothing for the engine. The laptop's `remote-upload-5k` gives Windows' share of frames by class of core beside it, which is evidence for row 233 | 3 |
| The snapshot's fill (D164, lane `snapshot-order`). `present-impl`'s fill builds a list of every presented row for each copied column, sorts each list by key, copies the bytes and sorts the keys once more, at every pass after a tick: 1.08 ms of a 1.72 ms pass at 10,000 presented rows in `measure-m2`'s runs, whatever the dirty count. In the probe of the proposal's review (a counting null sink, loads 3.7 to 4.6, steering) that half is 1.01 to 1.15 ms at 10,000 rows laid out as `measure-m2`'s scene lays them, 70 to 77% of it the sorts, and 0.28 to 0.39 ms where the keys lie in chunk order, as in `present-impl`'s cost scene (row 192); at 5,000 rows it is 0.52 to 0.56 ms, a pass of about 1.2 ms on the lane's 0.65 ms ring. The lane sorts the presented rows' places once, by key, and keeps the sorted places, each key with its chunk and row, for the next pass: a pass checks each place against the chunk's key as it copies, and sorts again only when a place no longer holds its key or the count of presented rows changed. A place says where a row is, not what it holds: the columns a row is copied from are read from its chunk at every pass. It is a hint and not a key (architecture section 8.2): no byte of the snapshot depends on it, and every buffer still holds the whole state (D157). The probe's prototype read 0.09 to 0.14 ms at 5,000 rows and 0.18 to 0.22 ms at 10,000, on every layout, and 0.23 and 0.44 ms with a sort at every pass. Owed by the lane: a test that compares the buffer each pass wrote with `present-impl`'s fill of the same world, over random sequences of moves, spawns, despawns, extracted components added and removed, region loads and unloads, restores and `set_tick`, under the reader cadences of the triple buffer's test, at the production layout, at a chunk capacity alone and under every layout mode SOUR runs; the count of passes that sorted, asserted to be none in a steady scene where the layout keeps rows in place, and recorded, not asserted, under SOUR's modes other than production, which permute rows at every sync point and so sort at every pass; the recorded test's hashes unchanged; and in `extract-50k` the snapshot's part at the plan's scene, at 10,000 presented rows and for a pass that sorts. The lane's target for the part at the plan's scene is 0.2 ms on the M1; the criterion is the pass's. If the part is over the target, the next step is to rewrite in each buffer only the rows stamped since that buffer was last written, which the planner sizes then. **Closed with this merge** (branch `snapshot-order`): the fill is as the row states. `ExtractState` keeps the presented rows' places, each a key, its chunk's index among the pass's presented chunks and its row, in key order; a pass checks every place against its chunk's key and their count against the presented rows, sorts again from the chunks when either fails, and reads each copied column from its chunk at every pass. The fill reads the places only through the order settled on the pass's chunks, so a fill from places not settled on them does not compile, and pushes each row's key with its bytes. No public signature changed. Held by the library's unit tests (`crates/present-api/src/extract/tests.rs`; the count of sorts is private): `the_fill_writes_what_present_impls_writes` rebuilds `present-impl`'s fill (a list per column, each sorted, and the keys sorted again) of the same world at the same point of every pass and compares the buffer the pass wrote with it, and whether the pass wrote with whether that fill would; at a pass that writes nothing, the snapshot left standing must still be that fill of the world, which holds the rule for idle passes without resting on the reference sharing it. It runs in a `Server`, a `Client` and a `Prediction` world, over random moves, spawns, despawns, extracted components added and removed, `Teleported` gained and lost at a row's chunk and row, region unloads and loads, restores and `set_tick` forward and back, under the five reader cadences of the triple buffer's test and a drawn one, two seeds each, at the production layout, at 1, 7 and 64 rows a chunk alone and under SOUR's `Rows(1)`, `Rows(7)` and `Rows(64)` with two seeds and `Bytes(16384)` with one: 1,682 passes a layout, 1,553 of them writing, no mismatch, with 1,078 to 1,082 sorts at the production layout and a capacity alone, where the script changes the rows at most frames, and 1,409 to 1,551 under SOUR's modes. `a_row_that_changes_archetype_in_place_is_read_from_its_chunk` finds a row that gains `Teleported` at its own chunk and row copied with it, its place holding and nothing sorted; `one_place_that_no_longer_holds_its_key_sorts_again` replaces the lowest key alone in its chunk, then the highest, and finds exactly one sort each; `a_steady_scene_sorts_nothing_where_the_layout_keeps_rows_in_place` finds 0 sorts in 30 steady passes of 200 presented rows, half of them moved, at the production layout and at 1, 7 and 64 rows a chunk alone, one each after a spawn, a despawn and an archetype change and none after a move to another region (a region is a column of the chunk); under each of SOUR's four modes it printed 30 sorts in 30 passes, not asserted. Four mutants, each undone after, fail a test: columns read as the chunk's were at the pass that last sorted (the equality test and the archetype test), a place check that skips the first place or the last (the one-place test and the equality test), no sort again on a change of count (all four), and the idle pass's check of the log's entries removed from the fill and the reference alike (the equality test, by its idle-pass check). SOUR's workers, layout and chaos modes passed at 88cb6a8, before the places were held through `Settled`, and its pull-request run at 88cb6a8, 1df7c3a and f22485f, with the recorded tests' hashes unchanged and identical across the three; those tests hash the world, not the snapshot. `crates/present-api/tests/cost.rs` is row 192's, rescened: at load averages 9.14 10.22 8.04 before and 9.37 10.24 8.07 after, one run, 60 passes an arm, the medians: at 5,000 presented rows, one worker, 0.93 ms without the snapshot, 1.01 ms with it (its part 0.08 ms) and 1.13 ms with one whose order breaks at every tick (0.20 ms); four workers 0.48, 0.62 (0.13 ms) and 0.73 ms (0.24 ms). At 10,000: one worker 0.92, 1.10 (0.17 ms) and 1.37 ms (0.45 ms); four workers 0.48, 0.74 (0.26 ms) and 0.92 ms (0.44 ms). A second run at load averages 19.51 27.62 28.11 before and 19.31 27.44 28.04 after: at 5,000, one worker 0.96, 1.03 (0.06 ms) and 1.33 ms (0.36 ms), four workers 0.52, 0.68 (0.16 ms) and 0.77 ms (0.25 ms); at 10,000, one worker 1.07, 1.20 (0.14 ms) and 1.35 ms (0.29 ms), four workers 0.50, 0.74 (0.24 ms) and 1.05 ms (0.55 ms). At the fix round's head, whose fill pushes each row through a callback, one run at load averages 18.84 18.34 14.99 before and after: at 5,000, four workers 0.48, 0.51 (0.03 ms) and 0.60 ms (0.12 ms), one worker unreadable, the arm without the snapshot slower than the arm with it; at 10,000, one worker 0.91, 1.07 (0.16 ms) and 1.35 ms (0.44 ms), four workers 0.47, 0.69 (0.22 ms) and 0.79 ms (0.32 ms). The snapshot's half is serial, so with four workers its part is a serial tail and reads larger than with one; the part is a difference of two medians and moves with the load. The review's baseline on the same scene, `present-impl`'s fill swapped back in (release, load averages 9.98 before and 9.58 after): its part 0.47 ms on one worker and 0.41 ms on four at 5,000 presented rows, 1.01 ms and 0.97 ms at 10,000. `extract-50k` (`crates/app-client/tests/extract.rs`) presents 5,000 rows, one in twenty of the moved and one in twenty of the rest, their keys alternating between the two presented archetypes, and prints its two null arms beside: `cargo xtask measure extract-50k` at commit f22485f on the M1 under MoltenVK 1.4.2, no layer, the ring in memory type 1 of 3 (DEVICE_LOCAL + HOST_VISIBLE + HOST_COHERENT + HOST_CACHED), 60 passes an arm after a full one, at load averages 9.64 8.97 6.39 before and 9.11 8.88 6.37 after (the lane's run, under the GPU lock, not the record): at 5,000 presented rows on 4 workers the pass's median 670.9 µs on the device, of it the ring's part 176.2 µs and the snapshot's 92.5 µs, and 680.8 µs on null, the snapshot's part 109.6 µs; on one worker 1,421.2 µs on the device, the ring alone 1,321.4 µs, 26.4 ns a dirty row; no wait for a free region. Beside it on null, on 4 workers, not judged: at 10,000 presented rows the pass 768.0 µs, the ring alone 585.4 µs, the snapshot's part 182.7 µs; and a pass that sorts, one presented row despawned and another spawned every tick, 782.7 µs against the ring alone's 568.8 µs, the snapshot's part 213.8 µs. The part at the plan's scene is under the lane's target of 0.2 ms on the device and on null, at that load; the record is the quiet window's file | 2 |
